A_{3}(i-2)\cup A_{3}(i-1)
such that
|N(v)\cap B_{i-2}|
is minimum. Then
N(v)\setminus B_{i}\subseteq B_{i-1}\cup B_{i-2}
. Suppose that
N(v)\setminus B_{i}
contains two non-adjacent vertices
s,t
with
s\in B_{i-1}
and
t\in B_{i-2}
. By the choice of
v
,
s
has a neighbor
r
in
A_{3}(i-1)
or
A_{3}(i-2)
. By Lemma
8.1
(3),
r\in A_{3}(i-2)
.

By Lemma
11.46
,
A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
. If
A_{2}(i)=\emptyset
, we are done by symmetry.
So
A_{2}(i)
contains a vertex
w
. If
w
has a neighbor
w^{\prime}\in A_{1}(i)
, then
v-s-r-q_{i+2}-N_{B_{i+1}}(w)-w-w^{\prime}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
w
is anticomplete to
A_{1}(i)
.
By Lemma
11.44
,
w
has two non-adjacent neighbors
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i+1)
. Then
v-s-r-N_{B_{i+2}}(a)-a-w-b
is an induced
P_{7}
.
This proves that
N(v)\setminus B_{i}
is a clique and the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 11.55
.

For any two non-adjacent vertices
t_{1},t_{2}\in T
,
N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i)
and
N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i)
are disjoint and
(N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i))\cup(N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i))\cup B_{i}
is a clique.

Proof.

Since
q_{i+2}
is a common neighbor of
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
and
G
is
C_{4}
-free,
N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i)
and
N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i)
are disjoint. If
s_{j}\in N(t_{j})\cap A_{1}(i)
are not adjacent, then
t_{1}-s_{1}-B_{i}-s_{2}-t_{2}-q_{i+2}-t_{1}
is an induced cyclic sequence. So
N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i)
and
N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i)
are complete. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free,
N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i)
and
N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i)
are clique.

Now suppose
b\in B_{i}
has a neighbor
s_{1}\in N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i)
. If
b
has a non-neighbor
s_{2}\in N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i)
, then
b-s_{1}-s_{2}-t_{2}-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-b
is an induced
C_{6}
. So
b
is complete to
N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i)
. It follows that
b
is complete to
N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i)
. So each vertex in
B_{i}
is either complete or anticomplete to
(N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i))\cup(N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i))
. If there is a vertex
b\in B_{i}
anticomplete to
(N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i))\cup(N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i))
, then
s_{1}-s_{2}-t_{2}-q_{i-2}-q_{i-1}-b-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. This proves that
B_{i}
is complete to
(N(t_{1})\cap A_{1}(i))\cup(N(t_{2})\cap A_{1}(i))
.
∎

Lemma 11.56
.

T
is a blowup of
P_{3}
. This implies that
\alpha(T)\leq 2
.

Proof.

If
T
is a clique, then there is nothing to prove. So
T
is not a clique and so
A_{3}(i-2)\cup A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
by Lemma
11.52
.

(1) Each vertex in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
is universal in
T\cup T_{0}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
.

By Lemmas
4.8
and
4.9
,
B_{i+2}\cup B_{i-2}
is a clique complete to
T
. If
T_{0}
is empty, we are done.
So
T_{0}
is not empty. By Lemma
11.48
, there are two non-adjacent vertices
a,b\in T
complete to
T_{0}
. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free,
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
is universal in
T\cup T_{0}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
. This proves (1).

(2)
T
cannot contain three pairwise non-adjacent vertices simplicial in
T\cup T_{0}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
.

Suppose that
T
contains three pairwise non-adjacent vertices
v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}
simplicial in
T\cup T_{0}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
. For
j=1,2,3
, we have
|N(v_{j})\cap A_{1}(i)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
It follows from Lemmas
11.55
and
11.54
that
(N(v_{1})\cap A_{1}(i))\cup(N(v_{2})\cap A_{1}(i))\cup(N(v_{3})\cap A_{1}(i))\cup B_{i}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction. This proves (2).

(3) Let
T^{\prime}\subseteq T
be cutset of
G[T]
such that each vertex in
T^{\prime}
is universal in
A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
. Then for each component
T^{\prime}
-component
K
of
G[T]
, there is a vertex
v_{K}
in
K\setminus T^{\prime}
that is simplicial in
K\cup T_{0}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
.

If
K\cup T_{0}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T^{\prime}
is a clique, there is nothing to prove. If
K\cup T_{0}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
is not a clique, then
K\cup T_{0}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
contains two non-adjacent simplicial vertices
s
and
s^{\prime}
by Lemma
11.4
. Since each vertex in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T^{\prime}
is universal in
K\cup T_{0}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
, we have
s,s^{\prime}\in(K\setminus T^{\prime})\cup T_{0}
. Since
T_{0}
is a clique by Lemma
11.51
, one of
s,s^{\prime}
is in
K\setminus T^{\prime}
. This proves (3).

By (2) and (3),
T
has at most two components. Next we show the following.

(4)
Let
L_{1},L_{2}\subseteq T
be two connected subgraphs such that
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are disjoint and anticomplete, and each vertex in
T\setminus(L_{1}\cup L_{2})
is universal in
A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
. Then
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are cliques.

Let
T^{\prime}=T\setminus(L_{1}\cup L_{2})
.
Suppose first that
T_{0}=\emptyset
. By the assumption of (4),
every simplicial vertex of
L_{i}
is simplicial in
A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
.
By (2),
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are cliques.
So we assume that
T_{0}\neq\emptyset
. By Lemma
11.48
, there exist two non-adjacent vertices
a,b\in T
complete to
T_{0}
. Since each vertex in
T^{\prime}
is universal in
A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
,
a,b\in L_{1}\cup L_{2}
.

Suppose first that
a,b
belong to the same
L_{i}
, say
L_{1}
. Since
L_{1}\cup T^{\prime}\cup T_{0}
is not a clique, it contains two non-adjacent vertices
s,s^{\prime}
simplicial in
L_{1}\cup T^{\prime}\cup T_{0}
by Lemmas
11.4
and
2.4
. By our assumption on
T^{\prime}
,
s,s^{\prime}\in L_{1}
. By
L_{1}
is anticomplete to
L_{2}
and (1),
s,s^{\prime}
are simplicial in
A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
. By (3),
L_{2}
contains a vertex
t
simplicial in
A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
. Then
s,s^{\prime},t
contradicts (2).

So we assume that
a\in L_{1}
and
b\in L_{2}
.
Let
u\in L_{1}
be a simplicial vertex of
L_{1}
.
Choose a simplicial vertex
s\in N_{L_{1}}[u]
with
|N(s)\cap T_{0}|
is minimum.
Suppose that
s
is not simplicial in
T^{\prime}\cup L_{1}\cup T_{0}
.
Then
s
has two non-adjacent vertices
p\in L_{1}
and
q\in T_{0}
.
If
p
is not simplicial in
L_{1}
, then
p
has a neighbor
r\in L_{1}
not adjacent to
s
.
It follows that
r-p-s-q-b-(N(b)\cap A_{1}(i))-B_{i}
is an induced sequence.
So
p
is simplicial in
L_{1}
.
By the choice of
s
,
p
has a neighbor
q^{\prime}\in T_{0}
not adjacent to
s
.
It follows that
p-q^{\prime}-q-s-p
is an induced
C_{4}
.
So
s
is simplicial in
T^{\prime}\cup T_{0}\cup L_{1}
.
By Lemma
2.6
, (2) and (3),
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are cliques. This proves (4).

If
T
has two components, then each component is a clique by applying (4) with
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
being the two components of
T
. So
T
is connected.
Let
x,y\in T
with
xy\notin E(G)
. If
p\in N(x)\cap A_{1}(i)
and
q\in N(y)\cap A_{1}(i)
are not adjacent, then
p-B_{i}-q-y-B_{i+2}-x-p
is an induced cyclic sequence. If there is an induced
P_{5}=a-b-c-d-e
with
a,b,c,d\in T
and
e\in A_{1}(i)
, then
a-b-c-d-e-N_{B_{i}}(e)-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. By Lemma
4.9
,
q_{i+2}
is complete to
T
. So all three conditions of Lemma
2.8
are satisfied for
X=T
,
Y=A_{1}(i)
and
u=q_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
2.8
,
T
is
P_{4}
-free and so
T
can be partitioned into two subsets
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
such that
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
are complete. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free, we may assume that
T_{1}
is a clique. We choose such a partition
(T_{1},T_{2})
so that
|T_{1}|
is maximum. It follows that
T_{2}
is disconnected. Since
A_{3}(i-2)\cup A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
and
T_{1}
is a universal clique in
T
,
T_{1}
is complete to
T_{0}
. It follows that each component of
T_{2}
has a vertex simplicial in
A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
.
By (2),
T_{2}
has exactly two components. By applying (4) to the two components of
T_{2}
, each component of
T_{2}
is a clique. So
T
is a blowup of
P_{3}
.
∎

Lemma 11.57
.

T
is complete to
T_{0}
.

Proof.

If
T_{0}=\emptyset
, there is nothing to prove. Let us assume that
T_{0}
is not empty. By Lemmas
11.51
and
11.48
,
T_{0}
is complete to two non-adjacent vertices
a,b
where
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
or
a,b\in T
. If
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
, then
T\cup T_{0}
is complete to
\{a,b\}
by Lemma
11.47
and so
T\cup T_{0}
is a clique.

So
a,b\in T
. Suppose for a contradiction that
c\in T
is not adjacent to
v\in T_{0}
. Then
c\neq a,b
. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free,
c
is not adjacent to
a
or
b
. If
c
is adjacent to
a
, then
c-a-v-b-(N(b)\cap A_{1}(i))-B_{i}-q_{i+1}
is an induced sequence. So
c
is anticomplete to
\{a,b\}
. Then
\{a,b,c\}
is an independent set in
T
, contradicting Lemma
11.56
.
∎

Lemma 11.58
.

T_{0}
is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
.

Proof.

If
T_{0}
is empty, there is nothing to prove. Let
v\in T_{0}
. By Lemma
11.48
, there are two vertices
a,b
complete to
T_{0}
where
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
or
a,b\in T
. If
a,b\in T
, then
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T_{0}
is complete to
\{a,b\}
and so we are done. So assume that
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
.

By Lemmas
11.40
and
11.47
,
T_{0}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. Suppose for a contradiction that
u\in B_{i-2}
is not adjacent to
v
. Then
u\neq a,b
. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free,
u
is not adjacent to
a
or
b
. If
u
is adjacent to
a
, then
u-a-v-b-(N(b)\cap B_{i-1})-q_{i}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
u
is anticomplete to
\{a,b\}
. In particular,
a,b\in A_{3}(i-2)
.

Choose a vertex
w\in B_{i-2}
anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-2)
. By Lemma
8.11
,
w
is anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-1)
.
By Lemma
11.46
and
A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset
, we have
A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
. By Lemma
11.41
,
w
has no neighbor in
A_{2}\setminus A_{2}(i+2)
.
Then
N(w)\setminus B_{i-1}\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T\cup T_{0}
. As
T_{0}\cup B_{i+2}
is complete to
\{a,b\}
,
T_{0}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique. Suppose that
v^{\prime},u\in N(w)\setminus B_{i-1}
are not adjacent. By Lemma
11.57
,

u\in B_{i-2}
and
v^{\prime}\in T_{0}
and so
u-w-v^{\prime}-a-N_{B_{i-1}}(a)-q_{i}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. This proves that
N(w)\setminus B_{i-1}
is a clique and so
|N(w)\cap B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Since
a,b\in A_{3}(i-2)
are not adjacent, there exist two non-adjacent vertices
t_{1},t_{2}\in A_{3}(i-2)
such that
|N(t_{j})\cap B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
for
j=1,2
by Lemma
11.53
. By Lemmas
11.54
and
8.10
,
B_{i}\cup(N(t_{1})\cap B_{i-1})\cup(N(t_{2})\cap B_{i-1})\cup(N(w)\cap B_{i-1})
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.
∎

Lemma 11.59
.

A_{1}(i)\setminus N(A_{2}(i+2))=\emptyset
. In other words, each vertex in
A_{1}(i)
has a neighbor in
A_{2}(i+2)
.

Proof.

Suppose not. Let
X=N(A_{2}(i+2))\cap A_{1}(i)
.
We first show that
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
is a clique. Suppose for a contradiction that
K_{1},K_{2}
are two components of
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
. By Lemma
11.6
, there are two non-adjacent vertices
a_{1},b_{1}\in X
complete to
K_{1}
and two non-adjacent vertices
a_{2},b_{2}\in X
complete to
K_{2}
. Let
v_{1}
be a vertex in
K_{1}
.
Since
v_{1}\notin K_{2}
,
v_{1}
is not complete to
\{a_{2},b_{2}\}
. If
v_{1}
is adjacent to
a_{2}
, then
v_{1}-a_{2}-K_{2}-b_{2}-(N(b_{2})\cap T)-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. This proves that
K_{1}
is anticomplete to
\{a_{2},b_{2}\}
. By symmetry,
K_{2}
is anticomplete to
\{a_{1},b_{1}\}
. It follows that
a_{1},b_{1},a_{2},b_{2}
are four pairwise distinct vertices. Let
b_{1}^{\prime}\in N(b_{1})\cap T
and
b_{2}^{\prime}\in N(b_{2})\cap T
.

Next, we show that
\{a_{1},b_{1},a_{2},b_{2}\}
is a stable set. Suppose for a contradiction that
a_{1}a_{2}\in E(G)
. Since
a_{1}
is anticomplete to
K_{2}
and
a_{2}
is anticomplete to
K_{1}
, we have
a_{1}b_{2},a_{2}b_{1}\notin E(G)
. Since
a_{1}-a_{2}-K_{2}-b_{2}-N_{T}(b_{2})-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is not an induced
P_{7}
,
a_{1}
is complete to
N(b_{2})\cap T
. By symmetry,
a_{2}
is complete to
N(b_{1})\cap T
.
Then
a_{1}b_{2}^{\prime},a_{2}b_{1}^{\prime}\in E(G)
. Since
a_{1},b_{1}
have a common neighbor in
K_{1}
, the neighborhoods of
a_{1}
and
b_{1}
in
T
are disjoint and complete cliques and so
b_{1}^{\prime}b_{2}^{\prime}\in E(G)
. It follows that
a_{1}-a_{2}-b_{1}^{\prime}-b_{2}^{\prime}-a_{1}
is an induced
C_{4}
.
This proves that
\{a_{1},b_{1},a_{2},b_{2}\}
is stable.

If
b_{1}^{\prime}=b_{2}^{\prime}
, then
a_{1},a_{2}
are not adjacent to
b_{1}^{\prime}
since
G
is
C_{4}
-free. Then
a_{1}-K_{1}-b_{1}-b_{1}^{\prime}-b_{2}-K_{2}-a_{2}
is an induced
P_{7}
. Therefore, each pair of vertices from
\{a_{1},b_{1},a_{2},b_{2}\}
has no common neighbors in
T
.
Then
a_{1}-K_{1}-b_{1}-\{b_{1}^{\prime},q_{i+2},b_{2}^{\prime}\}-b_{2}-K_{2}-a_{2}
is an induced sequence.
This proves that
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
is connected. By Lemma
11.6
,
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
is a clique.

Let
u\in A_{1}(i)\setminus X
.
By Lemma
11.6
, there are two non-adjacent vertices
a,b\in X
complete to
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
. If
v\in B_{i}
that is not adjacent to
a
, then
b-u-a-(N(a)\cap T)-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-v
is a bad
P_{7}
. So
a,b
are complete to
B_{i}\cup(A_{1}(i)\setminus X)
and
B_{i}\cup(A_{1}(i)\setminus X)
is a clique. Suppose that
v\in N(a)\cap X
is not adjacent to
v^{\prime}\in A_{1}(i)\setminus X
. It follows that
v^{\prime}b\in E(G)
and so
vb\notin E(G)
.
Since
v
and
b
have a common neighbor in
B_{i}
,
v-a-v^{\prime}-b-(N(b)\cap T)-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
N(a)\cap X
is complete to
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
.
By symmetry,
\{v,b\}
is complete to
B_{i}
.
So
N(a)\cap X
is complete to
B_{i}\cup(A_{1}(i)\setminus X)
.

We choose a non-adjacent pair
\{a_{0},b_{0}\}
of vertices in
X
complete to
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
such that
|N(a_{0})\cap X|+|N(b_{0})\cap X|
is minimum.
Next we show that
N(a_{0})\setminus T
is a clique. If
a_{0}
has a neighbor
t\in A_{3}(i-1)
, then
a_{0}-t-(N(t)\cap B_{i-2})-(N(a_{0})\cap T)-a_{0}
is an induced
C_{4}
. So
a_{0}
is anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-1)\cup A_{3}(i+1)
.
By Lemma
11.45
, we may assume that
A_{2}(i)=\emptyset
and so
N(a)\setminus T\subseteq B_{i}\cup A_{1}(i)\cup A_{2}(i-1)
.
Let
s,s^{\prime}
be two non-adjacent neighbors of
a_{0}
in
B_{i}\cup A_{1}(i)\cup A_{2}(i-1)
. By Proposition
6.1
,
N(a_{0})\cap(B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i-1))
is a clique and so we may assume that
s\in A_{1}(i)
. By Lemma
6.2
,
s^{\prime}\notin A_{2}(i-1)
and so
s^{\prime}\in B_{i}\cup A_{1}(i)
. Since
B_{i}\cup(A_{1}(i)\setminus X)
is a clique, we may assume that
s\in X
. Since
s
is complete to
B_{i}\cup(A_{1}(i)\setminus X)
, we have
s^{\prime}\in X
. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free,
b_{0}
is not adjacent to
s
or
s^{\prime}
, say
sb_{0}\notin E(G)
.
By the choice of
\{a_{0},b_{0}\}
,
s
has a neighbor
s^{\prime\prime}\in X
with
a_{0}s^{\prime\prime}\notin E(G)
.
Applying the claim
N(a)\cap X
is complete to
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
to the pair
\{s,b_{0}\}
, we have
s^{\prime\prime}
is complete to
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
. So
s^{\prime\prime}-s-a_{0}-s^{\prime}
is induced
P_{4}
, which contradicts Lemma
2.8
.

So
N(a_{0})\setminus T
is a clique and thus
|N(a_{0})\cap T|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. By symmetry,
|N(b_{0})\cap T|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Since
a_{0},b_{0}
have a common neighbor in
A_{1}(i)\setminus X
,
N(a_{0})\cap T
and
N(b_{0})\cap T
are disjoint and complete cliques. By Lemma
11.50
,
(N(a_{0})\cap T)\cup(N(b_{0})\cap T)\cup B_{i+2}\cup B_{i-2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 11.60
.

A_{1}(i)
is
P_{4}
-free.

Proof.

Suppose by contradiction that
a-b-c-d
is an induced
P_{4}
in
A_{1}(i)
. By Lemma
6.5
,
N(a)\cap B_{i},N(d)\cap B_{i}\subseteq N(b)\cap B_{i}=N(c)\cap B_{i}
. Suppose first that
a
and
d
have no common neighbors in
B_{i}
. Since
G
is
(C_{6},C_{7})
-free,
a
and
d
have neighborhood containment in
A_{2}(i+2)
, say
N(a)\cap T\subseteq N(d)\cap T
.
By Lemma
11.59
, there is a vertex
s\in N(a)\cap A_{2}(i+2)
. Then
sd\in E(G)
. Since
b,d
has a common neighbor
u\in B_{i}
,
sb\notin E(G)
and then
u-b-a-s-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-u
is an induced
C_{6}
. So
a,d
have a common neighbor
s\in B_{i}
and this vertex is complete to
a-b-c-d
.
But this contradicts Lemma
2.8
.
∎

Lemma 11.61
.

A_{1}(i)
is anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-1)\cup A_{3}(i+1)
. This implies that
A_{1}(i)
is anticomplete to
A_{3}
.

Proof.

Let
t\in A_{3}(i-1)
be adjacent to
a\in A_{1}(i)
. Let
b\in B_{i-1}
be a non-neighbor of
t
. By Lemma
11.59
,
a
has a neighbor
c\in T
. Then
b-q_{i-1}-t-a-c-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
∎

Lemma 11.62
.

For a component
K
of
A_{3}(i-1)
, let
s
be a
(K,B_{i})
-minimal simplicial vertex. Then
N(s)\setminus B_{i-2}
is a clique and so
|N(s)\cap B_{i-2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Proof.

By Lemma
11.61
,
N(s)\setminus B_{i-2}\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i-1}\cup A_{3}(i-1)\cup A_{2}(i-1)
. Suppose that
v,v^{\prime}\in N(s)\setminus B_{i-2}
are not adjacent. Suppose first that
v,v^{\prime}\notin A_{2}(i-1)
.
By Lemmas
8.1
and
8.3
,
v\in K
and
v^{\prime}\in B_{i}
.
By Lemma
2.7
, there is an induced
P_{4}=a-b-s-c
with
a,b\in K
and
c\in B_{i}
. Then
a-b-s-c-q_{i+1}-q_{i+2}-y
is an induced
P_{7}
.

Now we may assume that
v\in A_{2}(i-1)
and
v^{\prime}\in B_{i}\cup B_{i-1}\cup A_{3}(i-1)\cup A_{2}(i-1)
.
Suppose first that
v
has a neighbor
u\in A_{1}(i)
. By Lemma
11.59
,
u
has a neighbor
w\in T
. By Lemma
11.61
,
su\notin E(G)
. It follows that
v^{\prime}-s-v-u-w-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
v
has no neighbors in
A_{1}(i)
. By Lemma
11.44
,
v
has non-adjacent vertices
t_{1},t_{2}\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-1)
. Since
t_{1}-v-t_{2}-(N(t)\cap B_{i-2})-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-B_{i}
is not a bad
P_{7}
,
t_{2}
is complete to
B_{i}
. By symmetry,
t_{1}
is complete to
B_{i}
. We may assume that
s\neq t_{1}
. Note that both pairs
\{v^{\prime},t_{1}\}
and
\{s,t_{1}\}
have a common neighbor in
B_{i}
and so have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-2}
.
If
st_{1}\notin E(G)
, then
v^{\prime}-s-v-t_{1}-(N(t_{1})\cap B_{i-2})-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
st_{1}\in E(G)
. Since
t_{2}t_{1}\notin E(G)
, we have
s\neq t_{2}
. By symmetry,
st_{2}\in E(G)
. Since
s
is simplicial in
K
, we may assume that
t_{1}\in B_{i-1}
and
t_{2}\in A_{3}(i-1)
. Now
t_{1}\in B_{i-1}
mixes on an edge in
A_{3}(i-1)
, which contradicts Lemma
8.3
. This proves that
N(s)\setminus B_{i-2}
is a clique and so
|N(s)\cap B_{i-2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
∎

Lemma 11.63
.

A_{3}(i-1)
is anticomplete to
A_{2}(i-1)
. By symmetry,
A_{3}(i+1)
is anticomplete to
A_{2}(i)
.

Proof.

Suppose for a contradiction that
t\in A_{3}(i-1)
is adjacent to
v\in A_{2}(i-1)
. We first show that
t
is complete to
B_{i}
. Suppose first that
v
has a neighbor
u\in A_{1}(i)
.
By Lemma
11.61
,
ut\notin E(G)
. Since
u-v-t-(N(t)\cap B_{i-2})-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-B_{i}
is not a bad
P_{7}
,
t
is complete to
B_{i}
. Now suppose that
v
has no neighbors in
A_{1}(i)
. By Lemma
11.44
,
v
has two non-adjacent neighbors
t_{1},t_{2}\in A_{3}^{\prime}(i-1)
. Since
t_{1}-v-t_{2}-(N(t_{2})\cap B_{i-2})-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-B_{i}
is not a bad
P_{7}
, we have
t_{2}
is complete to
B_{i}
. By symmetry,
t_{1}
is complete to
B_{i}
. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free,
v
is complete to
B_{i}
.
By Lemma
4.8
,
t
is complete to
B_{i}
.

Let
K
be the component of
A_{3}(i-1)
containing
t
and
s\in K
be a
(K,B_{i})
-minimal simplicial vertex. By Lemma
11.62
,
|N(s)\cap B_{i-2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. By Lemma
11.50
, there exists
w\in B_{i-1}
be anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-1)
with
|N(w)\cap B_{i-2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. If
N(w)\cap B_{i-2}
and
N(s)\cap B_{i-2}
are disjoint, then
|B_{i-2}|>\frac{\omega}{2}
.
By Lemma
11.8
,
|T|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
By Lemma
11.50
,
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.

So
s
and
w
have a common neighbor in
B_{i-2}
and thus they have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
. Recall that
t
is complete to
B_{i}
. So
s\neq t
and thus
sv\notin E(G)
. Let
P=v_{0}-v_{1}-\cdots-v_{k}-v
be a shortest path such that
v_{0}=s
and
v_{i}\in K
for each
1\leq i\leq k
. We show that each
v_{i}
with
1\leq i\leq k
is complete to
B_{i}
. We have proved that
v_{k}
is complete to
B_{i}
. Suppose now that
v_{i}
is complete to
B_{i}
for
2\leq i\leq k
. If
v_{i-1}
has a non-neighbor
b\in B_{i}
, then
v_{i-2}-v_{i-1}-v_{i}-b-q_{i+1}-q_{i+2}-y
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
v_{i-1}
is complete to
B_{i}
. Therefore,
v_{1}
is complete to
B_{i}
. By Lemma
8.2
(3),
N(s)\cap B_{i-2}\subseteq N(v_{1})\cap B_{i-2}
. By Lemma
4.10
,
w
is anticomplete to
N(v_{1})\cap B_{i-2}
and so anticomplete to
N(s)\cap B_{i-2}
. This implies that
N(w)\cap B_{i-2}
and
N(s)\cap B_{i-2}
are disjoint, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 11.64
.

A_{2}(i-1)
is a clique complete to
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
. By symmetry,
A_{2}(i)
is a clique complete to
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i}
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
11.63
and Lemma
11.44
, each vertex in
A_{2}(i-1)
has a neighbor in
A_{1}(i)
. Suppose that
v\in A_{2}(i-1)
mixes on an edge
ab\in B_{i-1}
. Let
u\in A_{1}(i)
be a neighbor of
v
and
w\in A_{2}(i+2)
be a neighbor of
u
. Then
a-b-v-u-w-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
v
is complete to
B_{i-1}
. Suppose that
v
is not adjacent to
v^{\prime}\in A_{2}(i-1)\cup B_{i}
. Then
v^{\prime}-q_{i-1}-v-u-w-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is a bad
P_{7}
.
This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 11.65
.

If
A_{2}(i)\cup A_{2}(i-1)\neq\emptyset
, then
A_{1}(i)
is a clique.

Proof.

By symmetry, assume that
A_{2}(i)
contains a vertex
b
. By Lemmas
11.63
and
11.44
,
b
has a neighbor
a\in A_{1}(i)
. By Lemma
6.2
,
b
is complete to the component
K
of
A_{1}(i)
containing
a
. Suppose that
K
is not a clique.
By Lemmas
11.3
,
2.6
and

11.8
, there exist two non-adjacent simplicial vertices
s_{1},s_{2}
of
K
with
|N(s_{j})\cap T|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
for
j=1,2
.
Since
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
have a common
b\in A_{2}(i)
,
N(s_{1})\cap T
and
N(s_{2})\cap T
are disjoint and complete.
By Lemma
11.50
,
T\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So
K
is a clique. If
v\in K
is not adjacent to
u\in B_{i}
, then
u-b-v-N_{T}(v)-q_{i-2}-q_{i-1}-u
is an induced
C_{6}
by Lemma
11.64
. So
K
is complete to
B_{i}
. If
A_{1}(i)\neq K
, then there exist two non-adjacent simplicial vertices
s_{2}\in A_{1}(i)\setminus K
and
s_{1}\in K
with
|N(s_{j})\cap T|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
for
j=1,2
by Lemma
11.8
. Since
s_{1}
is complete to
K
,
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
hava a common neighbor in
B_{i}
and so
N(s_{1})\cap T
and
N(s_{2})\cap T
are disjoint and complete. Then
T\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.
∎

11.2.1
T
is not a clique

In this subsection,
T
is not a clique. Let
t_{1},t_{2}\in T
be two non-adjacent simplicial vertices of
T
.

Lemma 11.66
.

For
j=1,2
,
N(t_{j})\setminus A_{1}(i)
is a clique and so
K_{t_{j}}=N(t_{j})\cap A_{1}(i)
has size
>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. Moreover,
K_{t_{1}}
and
K_{t_{2}}
are disjoint and complete.

Proof.

By Lemma
11.52
,
N(t_{j})\setminus A_{1}(i)\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)
. By Lemmas
11.51
,
11.57
,
11.58
and
4.9
,
N(t_{j})\setminus A_{1}(i)
is a clique.
∎

Lemma 11.67
.

A_{1}(i)
is a clique.

Proof.

Suppose not. By Lemma
11.65
,
A_{2}(i-1)=A_{2}(i)=\emptyset
.
Let
a^{\prime}_{1},a^{\prime}_{2}
be two non-adjacent simplicial vertices of
A_{1}(i)
.
Choose
a_{j}\in N_{A_{1}(i)}[a^{\prime}_{j}]
simplicial in
A_{1}(i)
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}
and with minimal neighborhood in
T
for
j=1,2
.
By Lemma
11.8
,
N(a_{j})\setminus T
is a clique and so
|N(a_{j})\cap T|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

If
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
have a common neighbor in
B_{i}
, then
N(a_{1})\cap T
and
N(a_{2})\cap T
are disjoint and complete sine
G
is
(C_{4},C_{6})
-free, and so
(N(a_{1})\cap T)\cup(N(a_{2})\cap T)\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.
So
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
.
Next we show that
N(a_{j})\setminus B_{i}
is a clique. Note that
N(a_{j})\setminus B_{i}\subseteq A_{1}(i)\cup T
. Suppose that
s,s^{\prime}
are two non-adjacent vertices in
N(a_{j})\setminus B_{i}
. Then
s\in A_{1}(i)
and
s^{\prime}\in T
.
Suppose first that
s
is simplicial in
A_{1}(i)
.
By the minimality of
a_{1}
,
s
has a neighbor
w\in T\cup B_{i}
that is not adjacent to
a_{1}
.
If
w\in B_{i}
, then
w-s-a_{1}-s^{\prime}-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-w
is an induced
C_{6}
.
So
w\in T
and
N(s)\cap B_{i}\subseteq N(a_{1})\cap B_{i}
.
Then
s-a_{1}-s^{\prime}-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-N_{B_{i}}(a_{2})-q_{i-1}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
So
s
is no simplicial in
A_{1}(i)
.
Let
w
be a neighbor of
s
in
A_{1}(i)
that is not adjacent to
a_{1}
.
Then
w-s-a_{1}-s^{\prime}-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-N_{B_{i}}(a_{2})
is a bad
P_{7}
.
So
N(a_{1})\setminus B_{i}
is a clique and so
|N(a_{1})\cap B_{i}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
By symmetry,
|N(a_{2})\cap B_{i}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
By Lemmas
11.55
and
11.66
,
(N(a_{1})\cap B_{i})\cup(N(a_{2})\cap B_{i})\cup K_{t_{1}}\cup K_{t_{2}}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.
∎

Lemma 11.68
.

A_{3}(i+1)=A_{3}(i-1)=\emptyset

Proof.

By symmetry, we prove that
A_{3}(i+1)=\emptyset
. Suppose not. By Lemma
8.14
, there exists a vertex
v\in A_{3}(i+1)
such that
|N(v)\cap B_{i}|,|N(v)\cap B_{i+2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Let
w\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1))
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+2}
.
Suppose first that
w
and
v
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
11.50
,
|N(w)\cap B_{i+2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and so
(N(v)\cap B_{i+2})\cup(N(w)\cap B_{i+2})\cup B_{i-2}\cup T
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.
So
w
and
v
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
and so
B_{i}
is not complete to
B_{i+1}
.
By Lemma
11.64
,
A_{2}(i)=\emptyset
.
By Lemma
8.15
,
|N(w)\cap B_{i}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Then
(N(v)\cap B_{i})\cup(N(w)\cap B_{i})\cup K_{t_{1}}\cup K_{t_{2}}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
by Lemma
8.10
.
∎

Next, we give a lemma for reducing vertices of small degrees when we color
G
. This lemma will also be used in Section
12
.

Lemma 11.69
.

Let
X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*}
be four disjoint cliques of
G
with
|X_{1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
,
|X_{2}|\leq\frac{3}{4}\omega
and
|X_{3}|=\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
such that

•

X_{1}\cup X^{*}
is anticomplete to
X_{3}
,

•

every two vertices in
X_{1}\cup X^{*}
have comparable neighborhoods in
X_{2}
,

•

for every
v\in X^{*}
and every vertex
u\in X_{1}
,
N_{X_{2}}(v)\subseteq N_{X_{2}}(u)
,

•

P\subseteq X_{1}
is the set of
p
vertices with largest neighborhoods in
X_{2}
with
p\in\left[\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\right]
, and

•

F
is an induced subgraph of
G
with
F\cap P=\emptyset
.

If a vertex
v\in F\cap X_{2}
satisfies
N_{F}(v)\subseteq X_{1}\cup X_{2}\cup X_{3}\cup X^{*}
and
N(v)\cap(X_{1}\cup X_{2}\cup X^{*})
is a clique of
G
, then
d_{F}(v)<\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-p
.

Proof.

If
N_{F_{1}}(v)\cap X_{1}\neq\emptyset
, then
(N_{F_{1}}(v)\cap(X_{1}\cup X_{2}\cup X^{*}))\cup P
is also a clique of
G
by the definition of
P
.
This implies that
|N_{F_{1}}(v)\cap(X_{1}\cup X_{2}\cup X^{*})|\leq\omega-p
or
|N_{F_{1}}(v)\cap(X_{1}\cup X_{2}\cup X^{*})|=|N_{F_{1}}(v)\cap X_{2}|<\frac{3}{4}\omega
.
It follows that

\displaystyle|N_{F_{1}}(v)|

\displaystyle=|N_{F_{1}}(v)\cap X_{3}|+|N_{F_{1}}(v)\cap(X_{1}\cup X_{2}\cup X^{*})|

\displaystyle<\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil+\max\left\{\omega-p,\frac{3}{4}\omega\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-p.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 11.70
.

\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
11.45
and
11.41
, we may assume that
A_{2}=A_{2}(i)\cup A_{2}(i+2)
. By Lemmas
11.42

11.68
and
11.52
,
A_{3}=\emptyset
.

By Lemma
11.56
,
T
can be partitioned into three cliques
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}
such that
T_{2}
is complete to
T_{1}\cup T_{3}
and
T_{1}
and
T_{3}
are anticomplete where
T_{1},T_{3}\neq\emptyset
and
T_{2}
may be empty. Let
L=N(T_{1})\cap A_{1}(i)
,
R=N(T_{3})\cap A_{1}(i)
and
M=A_{1}(i)\setminus(L\cup R)
. Note that
L
and
R
are disjoint. We show that
T_{2}
is complete to
L\cup R
. Suppose that
v\in T_{2}
has a non-neighbor
u\in L\cup R
, say
u\in L
. If
v
is anticomplete to
L\cup R
, then
v
has a neighbor
v^{\prime}\in M
and so
v-v^{\prime}-u-N_{T_{1}}(u)-v
is an induced
C_{4}
. So
v
has a neighbor
w\in L\cup R
. If
w\in R
, then
w-u-N_{T_{1}}(u)-v-w
is an induced
C_{4}
. So
v
is anticomplete to
R
. By symmetry,
v
is anticomplete to
L
, a contradiction.

Next we give a
\left\lceil\frac{5\omega}{4}\right\rceil
-coloring of
G
.
Let
x=|B_{i-2}|
and
y=|B_{i+2}|
.
Our strategy is to use
p=x+y-2\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
colors to color some vertices of
G
first and then argue that the remaining vertices can be colored with
f=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-p
colors.
Note that

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-(x+y-2\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil)

\displaystyle=\omega+(3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-(x+y))

\displaystyle\geq\omega,

since
x+y\leq\frac{3}{4}\omega
.

Step 1: Precolor a subgraph.

Next we define vertices to be colored with
p
colors. Let
S_{i-2}\subseteq B_{i-2}
be the set of
x-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices,
S_{i+2}\subseteq B_{i+2}
be the set of
y-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices, and
S_{i}\subseteq B_{i}
be the set of
p
vertices with largest neighborhoods in
H
. Since
(x+y)-2\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil<|B_{i}|
, the choice of
S_{i}
is possible.
We color
S_{i}
with all colors in
[p]
, and color all vertices in
S_{i-2}\cup S_{i+2}
with all colors in
[p]
. Since
B_{i}
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
, this coloring is proper. So it suffices to show that

G_{1}=G-(S_{i}\cup S_{i-2}\cup S_{i+2})
is
f
-colorable.

Step 2: Reduce by degeneracy.

Next, we use Lemma
11.69
to further reduce the problem to color a subgraph of
G_{1}
.
Let
v\in B_{i-1}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=(B_{i},B_{i-1},B_{i-2}\setminus S_{i-2},\emptyset)
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}}(v)<f
.
It follows that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{1}-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
G_{1}-B_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable.

Next, we reduce vertices in
B_{i+1}
. By Lemmas
11.64
and
4.8
,
N_{B_{i+1}}(u^{\prime})=N_{B_{i+1}}(u)
for every
u\in B_{i}
and
u^{\prime}\in A_{2}(i)
.
Let
v\in B_{i+1}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i+2}\cup B_{i}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=(B_{i},B_{i+1},B_{i+2}\setminus S_{i+2},A_{2}(i))
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}-B_{i-1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}-B_{i-1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}-B_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
(G_{1}-B_{i-1})-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}-B_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
(G_{1}-B_{i-1})-B_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable.

Step 3: Reduce simplicial vertices.

Now it suffices to show that
G_{2}=G_{1}-B_{i-1}-B_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable.
Let
v\in B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i)
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
A_{1}(i)
.
Note that
v
is simplicial in
G_{2}
.
So
G_{2}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{2}-v
is
f
-colorable.
By applying this step repeatedly, we have
G_{2}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{2}-(B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i))
is
f
-colorable.

Let
v\in M
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
T_{2}
.
Note that
v
is simplicial in
G_{2}-(B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i))
.
So
G_{2}-(B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i))
is
f
-colorable if and only if
(G_{2}-(B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i)))-v
is
f
-colorable.
By applying this step repeatedly, we have
G_{2}-(B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i))
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{2}-(B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i))-M
is
f
-colorable.
Let
G_{3}=G_{2}-(B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i))-M
.

Step 4: Color
G_{3}
.

So it suffices to show that
G_{3}
is
f
-colorable.
Note that every vertex in
T_{2}
is universal in
G_{3}
.
So it suffices to show that
G_{4}=G_{3}-T_{2}
is
(f-|T_{2}|)
-colorable.
Let
H^{\prime}
be a subgraph of
G_{4}
which is hyperhole with maximum chromatic number.
It follows that
\omega(H^{\prime})\leq\omega-|T_{2}|\leq f-|T_{2}|
.
Since
L\cup R
is complete to
B_{i}
,
|L\cup R|<\omega-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Let
M_{j}=V(G_{4})\cap(T_{j}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T_{0})
for
j=1,3
.
Since
T_{1}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T_{0}
is complete to
T_{2}
,
|M_{1}|=|V(G_{4})\cap(T_{1}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T_{0})|\leq\omega-|T_{2}|-p
.
By symmetry,
|M_{3}|=|V(G_{4})\cap(T_{3}\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T_{0})|\leq\omega-|T_{2}|-p
.
Then

\displaystyle|V(H^{\prime})|\leq|V(G_{4})|

\displaystyle=|L\cup R|+|M_{1}|+|M_{3}|-|B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T_{0}|

\displaystyle<\left(\omega-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\right)+2(\omega-|T_{2}|-p)-2\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil

\displaystyle=3\omega+\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-2(x+y)-2|T_{2}|

\displaystyle<2\omega+6\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-2(x+y)-2|T_{2}|

\displaystyle=2(f-|T_{2}|),

By
[
12
]
(Theorem 1.2),
\chi(G_{4})=\chi(H^{\prime})=\max\left\{\omega(H^{\prime}),\left\lceil\frac{|V(H^{\prime})|}{2}\right\rceil\right\}\leq f-|T_{2}|
.
∎

11.2.2
T
is a clique

Lemma 11.71
.

If
A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset
, any vertex
w\in B_{i-2}
anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-2)
has
N(w)\setminus B_{i-1}
is a clique and so
|N(w)\cap B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Proof.

By Lemma
11.46
, we have
A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
.
By Lemma
8.11
,
w
is anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-1)
.
By Lemma
11.41
,
w
has no neighbor in
A_{2}\setminus A_{2}(i+2)
.
By Lemmas
11.57
,
11.58
,
11.51
and the assumption that
T
is a clique,
N(w)\setminus B_{i-1}=B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T\cup T_{0}
is a clique.
∎

Lemma 11.72
.

A_{3}(i-2)
is a clique. By symmetry,
A_{3}(i+2)
is a clique.

Proof.

Suppose not. Let
s,s^{\prime}\in A_{3}(i-2)
be two non-adjacent vertices simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
. By Lemma
11.53
,
|N(s)\cap B_{i-1}|,|N(s^{\prime})\cap B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. Let
w\in B_{i-2}
be anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-2)
. By Lemma
11.71
,
|N(w)\cap B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. It follows from
Lemmas
8.10
and
11.54
that
B_{i}\cup(N(s)\cap B_{i-1})\cup(N(s^{\prime})\cap B_{i-1})\cup(N(w)\cap B_{i-1})
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.
∎

Lemma 11.73
.

If
A_{3}(i-2)\cup A_{3}(i+2)\neq\emptyset
, then
A_{3}(i-1)\cup A_{3}(i+1)=\emptyset
.

Proof.

By symmetry, we assume that
A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset
. Let
t\in A_{3}(i-2)
be a vertex simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
and
b_{i-2}\in B_{i-2}
be a vertex anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-2)
. By Lemma
8.11
,
b_{i-2}
is anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-1)
. By Lemma
11.53
,
|N(t)\cap B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. By Lemma
11.71
,
|N(b_{i-2})\cap B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Let
s\in A_{3}(i-1)
and
b_{i-1}\in B_{i-1}
be anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-1)
. If
A_{2}(i-1)
contains a vertex
a
, then
s-(N(s)\cap B_{i-1})-a-(N(a)\cap A_{1}(i))-T-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is an induced sequence. So
A_{2}(i-1)=\emptyset
.
By Lemmas
8.14
and
8.15
, there are
v\in A_{3}(i-1)
and
w\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-1))
such that
N(v)\cap B_{i},N(v)\cap B_{i-2},N(w)\cap B_{i},N(w)\cap B_{i-2}
are cliques of size larger than
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
If
v
and
w
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
, then
(N(v)\cap B_{i})\cup(N(w)\cap B_{i})\cup(N(t)\cap B_{i-1})\cup(N(b_{i-2})\cap B_{i-1})
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
by Lemma
8.10
.
So
v
and
w
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-2}
.
Then
(N(v)\cap B_{i-2})\cup(N(w)\cap B_{i-2})\cup(N(t)\cap B_{i-1})\cup(N(b_{i-2})\cap B_{i-1})
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
by Lemma
8.13
.
∎

Lemma 11.74
.

If
A_{3}(i\pm 2)\neq\emptyset
, then
A_{1}(i)
is a clique.

Proof.

By symmetry, assume that
A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset
.
Suppose that
a\in A_{3}(i-2)
is simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
. Let
b\in B_{i-2}
anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-2)
.
If
c\in B_{i}
mixes on an edge
st\in A_{1}(i)
, then
a-q_{i+2}-b-q_{i-1}-c-s-t
is an induced
P_{7}
. So each vertex in
B_{i}
is complete or anticomplete to any component of
A_{1}(i)
.
If a component
K
of
A_{1}(i)
is not a clique, then
|T|>\frac{\omega}{2}
by Lemma
11.8
and so
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So any component of
A_{1}(i)
is a clique.

Suppose that
A_{1}(i)
has two components
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
. Note that
K_{a}=N(a)\cap B_{i-1}
and
K_{b}=N(b)\cap B_{i-1}
are disjoint and have size
>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
by Lemmas
11.71
and
11.53
. If
u\in L_{j}
has a neighbor
w\in A_{2}(i)
, then
u-w-(N(w)\cap B_{i+1})-q_{i+2}-b-K_{b}-K_{a}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
L_{j}
is anticomplete to
A_{2}(i)
for
j=1,2
. It follows that
N(L_{j})\subseteq B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i-1)\cup T
.
Let
s_{j}\in L_{j}
such that
|N(s_{j})\cap T|
is minimum. Since
L_{j}
and
T
are cliques,
N(s_{j})\cap(L_{j}\cup T)
is a clique. By Proposition
6.1
,
K_{s_{j}}=N(s_{j})\cap(B_{i}\cup A_{2}(i-1))=N(s_{j})\setminus(L_{j}\cup T)
is a clique and so
|K_{s_{j}}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. If
K_{s_{1}}
and
K_{s_{2}}
are not disjoint, then
|T|>\frac{\omega}{2}
and so
T\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So
K_{s_{1}}
and
K_{s_{2}}
are disjoint. Since
G
is
(C_{6},C_{7})
-free,
K_{s_{1}}
and
K_{s_{2}}
are complete.
By Lemma
8.10
and
11.64
,
K_{s_{1}}\cup K_{s_{2}}\cup K_{a}\cup K_{b}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. This proves that
A_{1}(i)
is a clique.
∎

Lemma 11.75
.

A_{3}(i-1)
or
A_{3}(i+1)
is empty.

Proof.

Suppose not.
By Lemmas
11.62
and
11.50
, there exist
s\in A_{3}(i-1)
and
a\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(s)
such that
|N(a)\cap B_{i-2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and
|N(s)\cap B_{i-2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, and there exist
t\in A_{3}(i+1)
and
b\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(t)
such that
|N(b)\cap B_{i+2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and
|N(t)\cap B_{i+2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
By Lemma
8.9
, we may assume that
N(a)\cap B_{i-2}
and
N(s)\cap B_{i-2}
are disjoint, and so
\omega(B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T)>\omega
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 11.76
.

A_{1}(i)
is a blowup of
P_{3}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
11.65
, we may assume that
A_{2}(i-1)=A_{2}(i)=\emptyset
and
A_{1}(i)
is not a clique.

(1) Any two non-adjacent
(A_{1}(i),B_{i})
-minimal simplicial vertices
a,b
have a common neighbor in
T
.

Suppose not. By Lemma
11.8
,
|N(a)\cap T|,|N(b)\cap T|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. It follows that
\omega(T\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2})>\omega
, a contradiction. This proves (1).

(2) If there are two non-adjacent simplicial vertices
a,b
of
A_{1}(i)
which have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
, then
|N(a)\cap B_{i}|,|N(b)\cap B_{i}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. Moreover,
(N(a)\cap B_{i})\cup(N(b)\cap B_{i})
is complete to
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+1}
.

It suffices to show that
N(a)\setminus B_{i}
and
N(b)\setminus B_{i}
are cliques. Note that
N(a)\setminus B_{i}\subseteq A_{1}(i)\cup T
.
Since
a
is simplicial in
A_{1}(i)
and
T
is a clique, it suffices to show that
N(a)\cap A_{1}(i)
is complete to
N(a)\cap T
. Suppose that
s\in N(a)\cap A_{1}(i)
is not adjacent to
s^{\prime}\in N(a)\cap T
. If
s
has a neighbor
p\in B_{i}
not adjacent to
a
, then
p-s-a-s^{\prime}-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-p
is an induced
C_{6}
. So
N(s)\cap B_{i}\subseteq N(a)\cap B_{i}
. Then
s-a-s^{\prime}-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-N_{B_{i}}(b)-q_{i-1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. This proves that
N(a)\setminus B_{i}
is a clique. By symmetry,
N(b)\setminus B_{i}
is a clique.

We first show that each vertex
v\in B_{i-1}
is either complete or anticomplete to
(N(a)\cap B_{i})\cup(N(b)\cap B_{i})
. Suppose that
v
has a neighbor
a^{\prime}\in N(a)\cap B_{i}
. If
v
has a non-neighbor
b^{\prime}\in N(b)\cap B_{i}
, then
v-a^{\prime}-b^{\prime}-b-N_{T}(b)-q_{i-2}-v
is an induced
C_{6}
. So
v
is complete to
N(b)\cap B_{i}
. It follows that
v
is complete to
N(a)\cap B_{i}
. Now suppose that
v
is anticomplete to
(N(a)\cap B_{i})\cup(N(b)\cap B_{i})
. Let
c\in B_{i}
be a neighbor of
v
and
d\in N(a)\cap B_{i}
. Then
v-c-d-a-N_{T}(a)-q_{i-2}-v
is an induced
C_{6}
.
This proves (2).

(3)
|B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
or
|B_{i+1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

By Lemma
11.71
, we may assume that
A_{3}(i-2)=A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
.
By Lemma
11.75
, we may assume that
A_{3}(i-1)=\emptyset
. For any vertex
v\in B_{i-2}
,
N(v)\setminus B_{i-1}=A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i+2}\cup B_{i-2}
is a clique since
T
is a clique.
It follows that
|B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
This proves (3).

By (1), (2), (3) and Lemma
2.6
,
A_{1}(i)
cannot have three pairwise non-adjacent simplicial vertices. This implies that
A_{1}(i)
has at most two components and if
A_{1}(i)
has two components, then each component is a clique. So assume that
A_{1}(i)
is connected. Since
A_{1}(i)
is
P_{4}
-free,
A_{1}(i)
can be partitioned into two non-empty subsets
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
such that
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
are complete. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free,
X_{1}
is a clique. Choose such a partition
(X_{1},X_{2})
such that
|X_{1}|
is maximum. It follows that
X_{2}
is disconnected.
Since each vertex simplicial in
X_{2}
is simplicial in
A_{1}(i)
,
X_{2}
has exactly two clique components. So
A_{1}(i)
is a blowup of
P_{3}
.
∎

Lemma 11.77
.

A_{3}(i-1)
is a clique and for every vertex
a\in A_{3}(i-1)
,
N_{B_{i}}(a)
is anticomplete to
B_{i-1}\setminus N(a)
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
A_{3}(i-1)
is not a clique.
By Lemma
11.73
,
A_{3}(i-2)=A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
.
If
A_{2}(i-1)
contains a vertex
p
, let
s\in A_{3}(i-1)
and then
s-(N(s)\cap B_{i-1})-p-(N(p)\cap A_{1}(i))-T-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}
is an induced sequence. So
A_{2}(i-1)=\emptyset
.
By Lemmas
2.6
,
8.14
and
8.15
, there exist three pairwise non-adjacent vertices
v_{1},v_{2}\in A_{3}(i-1)
and
w\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-1))
such that
N(v_{1})\cap B_{i},N(v_{1})\cap B_{i-2},N(v_{2})\cap B_{i},N(v_{2})\cap B_{i-2},N(w)\cap B_{i},N(w)\cap B_{i-2}
are cliques of size larger than
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
If any two of
v_{1},v_{2},w
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-2}
, then
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup T
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.
So
v_{1},v_{2},w
have pairwise comparable neighborhoods in
B_{i-2}
and so have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
.
Let
v
be a vertex in
B_{i+2}
.
Since
N(v)\subseteq B_{i+1}\cup(B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2))
and
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)
is a clique,
|N(v)\cap B_{i+1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
By Lemmas
8.10
,
(N(v_{1})\cap B_{i})\cup(N(v_{2})\cap B_{i})\cup(N(w)\cap B_{i})\cup B_{i+1}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.
So
A_{3}(i-1)
is a clique.

Let
u
be a vertex in
A_{3}(i-1)
with minimal neighborhood in
H
.
Since
A_{3}(i-1)
is a clique,
u
is simplicial in
A_{3}(i-1)
.
By Lemma
8.14
,
|N(u)\cap B_{i-2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
By Lemma
8.15
, there exists
w\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-1))
such that
|N(w)\cap B_{i-2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
If
u
and
w
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-2}
, then
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.
So
w
and
v
have a common neighbor in
B_{i-2}
.
By the minimality of
u
, every vertex
a
in
A_{3}(i-1)
and
w
have a common neighbor in
B_{i-2}
, and so
N_{B_{i}}(a)
is anticomplete to
B_{i-1}\setminus N(a)
.
∎

Lemma 11.78
.

If
A_{3}(i\pm 1)\neq\emptyset
, then
A_{1}(i)
is a clique.

Proof.

Suppose for a contradiction that
A_{1}(i)
is not a clique.
By symmetry,
A_{3}(i-1)\neq\emptyset
.
Let
v\in A_{3}(i-1)
and
w\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-1))
.
By Lemma
11.77
,
v
and
w
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
.
By Lemma
11.8
,
A_{1}(i)
has two non-adjacent vertices
s_{1},s_{2}
such that
|N(s_{j})\cap T|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
for
j=1,2
. If
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
T
, then
\omega(T\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2})>\omega
. So
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
.
If there is an induced
P_{4}=a-b-c-d
such that
a\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-1)
,
b,c\in B_{i}
and
d\in A_{1}(i)
, then
a-b-c-d-N_{T}(d)-N_{B_{i-2}}(a)-a
is an induced
C_{6}
.
It follows that
N(v),N(w)\subseteq N(s_{j})
for
j=1,2
. This contradicts that
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
.
∎

Lemma 11.79
.

If
A_{3}\neq\emptyset
,
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

By Lemma
11.41
, we may assume that
A_{2}=A_{2}(i-1)\cup A_{2}(i)\cup A_{2}(i+2)
, where
A_{2}(i-1)
and
A_{2}(i)
may be empty.
By Lemmas
11.74
and
11.78
,
A_{1}(i)
is a clique.
If
A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset
, then by Lemmas
11.73
,
11.42
and
11.46
,
A_{3}=A_{3}(i-2)
and by Lemma
11.72
,
A_{3}(i-2)
is a clique. If
A_{3}(i-2)=A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
, then by Lemma
11.75
, we may assume that
A_{3}(i+1)=\emptyset
.
Moreover, by Lemma
11.77
,
A_{3}(i-1)
is a clique.

Next we give a
\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
-coloring of
G
.
Let

x=\begin{cases}\,|A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2))|,&\text{if }\,A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset,\\
\,|B_{i-2}|,&\text{if }\,A_{3}(i-2)=\emptyset,\end{cases}

y=|B_{i+2}|
, and
z=|T|
.
Our strategy is to use
p=x+y+z-3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
colors to color some vertices of
G
first and then argue that the remaining vertices can be colored with
f=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-p
colors.
Note that

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-(x+y+z-3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil)

\displaystyle=\omega+(4\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-(x+y+z))

\displaystyle\geq\omega,

since
x+y+z\leq\omega
.

Step 1: Precolor a subgraph.

Next we define vertices to be colored with
p
colors. Let

S_{i-2}\subseteq\begin{cases}\,A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2)),&\text{if }\,A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset,\\
\,B_{i-2},&\text{if }\,A_{3}(i-2)=\emptyset,\end{cases}

be the set of
x-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices,
S_{i+2}\subseteq B_{i+2}
be the set of
y-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices,
S_{T}\subseteq T
be the set of
z-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices, and

S_{i}\subseteq B_{i}
be the set of
p
vertices with largest neighborhoods in
H\cup A_{1}(i)
if
A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset
or with largest neighborhoods in
A_{3}(i-1)\cup B_{i+1}\cup A_{1}(i)
if
A_{3}(i-2)=\emptyset
. Since
(x+y+z)-3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil<|B_{i}|
, the choice of
S_{i}
is possible.
We color
S_{i}
with all colors in
[p]
, and color all vertices in
S_{i-2}\cup S_{i+2}\cup S_{T}
with all colors in
[p]
. Since
B_{i}
is anticomplete to
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)\cup B_{i+2}\cup T
, this coloring is proper. So it suffices to show that

G_{1}=G-(S_{i}\cup S_{i-2}\cup S_{i+2}\cup S_{T})
is
f
-colorable.

Step 2: Reduce by degeneracy.

Next, we use Lemma
11.69
to further reduce the problem to color a subgraph of
G_{1}
.
Suppose first that
A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset
.
Let
R_{i-1}
be the set of vertices in
B_{i-1}
that is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
. Let
v\in R_{i-1}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
among
R_{i-1}
. Since
v
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
,
v
is also minimal among
B_{i-1}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=\left(B_{i},B_{i-1},\left(A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}\left(A_{3}(i-2)\right)\right)\setminus S_{i-2},A_{2}(i-1)\right)
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{1}-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable.
Then we suppose that
A_{3}(i-2)=\emptyset
.
Let
R_{i-1}=A_{3}(i-1)
.
Let
v\in R_{i-1}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}
among
R_{i-1}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=(B_{i},A_{3}(i-1)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i-1)),B_{i-2}\setminus S_{i-2},A_{2}(i-1))
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{1}-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable.

Let
v\in B_{i+1}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i+2}\cup B_{i}
among
B_{i+1}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=(B_{i},B_{i+1},B_{i+2}\setminus S_{i+2},A_{2}(i))
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}-R_{i-1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}-R_{i-1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
(G_{1}-R_{i-1})-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
(G_{1}-R_{i-1})-B_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable.

Let
v\in A_{1}(i)
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup T
among
A_{1}(i)
.
By Lemma
11.45
, we may assume that
A_{2}(i)=\emptyset
.
Let
u\in B_{i}
and
u^{\prime}\in A_{2}(i-1)
.
If
u^{\prime}
has a neighbor
u^{\prime\prime}\in A_{1}(i)
with
uu^{\prime\prime}\notin E(G)
, then
u-(B_{i-1}\cup\{u^{\prime}\})-u^{\prime\prime}-T-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-u
is an induced cyclic sequence.
So for every
u\in B_{i}
and
u^{\prime}\in A_{2}(i-1)
,
N_{A_{1}(i)}(u^{\prime})\subseteq N_{A_{1}(i)}(u)
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=(B_{i},A_{1}(i),T\setminus S_{T},A_{2}(i-1))
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
(G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1})-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}-A_{1}(i)
is
f
-colorable.

Note that
G_{2}=G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}-A_{1}(i)
is chordal and so
\chi(G_{2})\leq\omega\leq f
.
∎

Lemma 11.80
.

If
A_{3}=\emptyset
,
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

By Lemma
11.41
, we may assume that
A_{2}=A_{2}(i-1)\cup A_{2}(i)\cup A_{2}(i+2)
, where
A_{2}(i-1)
and
A_{2}(i)
may be empty.
By Lemma
11.76
,
A_{1}(i)
can be partitioned into three cliques
L\cup M\cup R
such that
M
is complete to
L\cup R
and
R
and
L
are anticomplete where
L\neq\emptyset
. We may assume that if
R=\emptyset
, then
M=\emptyset
.
For any
v\in L
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}
,
N(v)\cap(A_{1}(i)\cup B_{i})
is also a clique by Lemma
6.5
.

Next we give a
\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
-coloring of
G
.
Let
x=|B_{i-2}|
,

y=|B_{i+2}|
, and
z=|T|
.
Our strategy is to use
p=x+y+z-3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
colors to color some vertices of
G
first and then argue that the remaining vertices can be colored with
f=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-p
colors.
Note that

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-(x+y+z-3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil)

\displaystyle=\omega+(4\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-(x+y+z))

\displaystyle\geq\omega,

since
x+y+z\leq\omega
.

Step 1: Precolor a subgraph.

Next we define vertices to be colored with
p
colors. Let

S_{i-2}\subseteq B_{i-2}
be the set of
x-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices,
S_{i+2}\subseteq B_{i+2}
be the set of
y-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices,
S_{T}\subseteq T
be the set of
z-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices, and

S_{i}\subseteq B_{i}
be the set of
p
vertices with largest neighborhoods in
H\cup L\cup M
. Since
(x+y+z)-3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil<|B_{i}|
, the choice of
S_{i}
is possible.
We color
S_{i}
with all colors in
[p]
, and color all vertices in
S_{i-2}\cup S_{i+2}\cup S_{T}
with all colors in
[p]
. Since
B_{i}
is anticomplete to
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)\cup B_{i+2}\cup T
, this coloring is proper. So it suffices to show that

G_{1}=G-(S_{i}\cup S_{i-2}\cup S_{i+2}\cup S_{T})
is
f
-colorable.

Step 2: Reduce by degeneracy.

Next, we use Lemma
11.69
to further reduce the problem to color a subgraph of
G_{1}
.
Let
R_{i-1}=B_{i-1}
.
Let
v\in R_{i-1}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}
among
R_{i-1}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=(B_{i},B_{i-1},B_{i-2}\setminus S_{i-2},A_{2}(i-1))
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{1}-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable.

Let
v\in B_{i+1}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i+2}\cup B_{i}
among
B_{i+1}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=(B_{i},B_{i+1},B_{i+2}\setminus S_{i+2},A_{2}(i))
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}-R_{i-1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}-R_{i-1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
(G_{1}-R_{i-1})-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
(G_{1}-R_{i-1})-B_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable.

Next, we reduce
R_{z}=L
.
Let
v\in L
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup T
among
A_{1}(i)
. So
N(v)\cap(A_{1}(i)\cup B_{i})
is a clique.
By Lemma
11.45
, we may assume that
A_{2}(i)=\emptyset
.
Let
u\in B_{i}
and
u^{\prime}\in A_{2}(i-1)
.
If
u^{\prime}
has a neighbor
u^{\prime\prime}\in A_{1}(i)
with
uu^{\prime\prime}\notin E(G)
, then
u-(B_{i-1}\cup\{u^{\prime}\})-u^{\prime\prime}-T-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-u
is an induced cyclic sequence.
So for every
u\in B_{i}
and
u^{\prime}\in A_{2}(i-1)
,
N_{A_{1}(i)}(u^{\prime})\subseteq N_{A_{1}(i)}(u)
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=(B_{i},L\cup M,T\setminus S_{T},A_{2}(i-1))
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
(G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1})-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}-R_{z}
is
f
-colorable.

Note that
G_{2}=G_{1}-R_{i-1}-B_{i+1}-R_{z}
is chordal and so
\chi(G_{2})\leq\omega\leq f
.
∎

12
A_{1}
is empty and
A_{2}
is not empty

Now we can assume that
A_{1}=\emptyset
. Let
K
be a non-empty component of
A_{2}(i+2)
.
By Lemma
10.1
,
K
has two non-adjacent neighbors
a,b
complete to
K
where
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
or
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i+2)
. We call
\{a,b\}
a
special pair
for
K
. Note that special pairs for
K
are not unique. By Lemma
4.14
,
A_{5}
is complete to
K
. Since
K
is arbitrary, each vertex in
A_{5}
is universal in
G
and so
A_{5}=\emptyset
.

Lemma 12.1

(Basic properties of
A_{2}(i)
)
.

Let
K
be a non-empty component of
A_{2}(i+2)
and
\{a,b\}\subseteq A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
be a special pair for
K
. Then
a,b
are complete to
B_{i+2}
and have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-1}
and so
K\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique. Moreover,
B_{i}
and
B_{i+1}
are complete.

Proof.

Let
v\in K
. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free,
a
and
b
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-1}
. Since
a-v-b-N_{B_{i-1}}(b)-q_{i}-q_{i+1}-B_{i+2}
is not a bad
P_{7}
,
b
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. By symmetry,
a
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. Since
K\cup B_{i+2}
is complete to
\{a,b\}
,
K\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique. If
c\in B_{i}
mixes on an edge
st\in B_{i+1}
, then
a-v-b-N_{B_{i-1}}(b)-c-s-t
is an induced
P_{7}
. This proves that
B_{i}
is complete to
B_{i+1}
.
∎

Lemma 12.2
.

A_{2}(i+2)
is anticomplete to either
A_{3}(i-2)
or
A_{3}(i+2)
.

Proof.

Suppose not. By Lemma
7.1
, let
K
and
K^{\prime}
be two components of
A_{2}(i+2)
such that
K
has a neighbor in
A_{3}(i-2)
and
K^{\prime}
has a neighbor in
A_{3}(i+2)
.

By Lemma
10.1
,
K
has two non-adjacent neighbors
a,b
complete to
K
where
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
and
K^{\prime}
has two non-adjacent neighbors
a’,b^{\prime}
complete to
K
where
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i+2)
. We may assume that
b\in A_{3}(i-2)
and
b^{\prime}\in A_{3}(i+2)
.
By Lemma

7.1
,
K
is anticomplete to
b^{\prime}
and
K^{\prime}
is anticomplete to
b
. It follows that
K-b-N_{B_{i-1}}(b)-q_{i}-N_{B_{i+1}}(b^{\prime})-b^{\prime}-K^{\prime}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
∎

Lemma 12.3
.

A_{2}(i+2)
is a clique.

Proof.

Suppose not. Let
K
and
K^{\prime}
be two components of
A_{2}(i+2)
. By Lemmas
10.1
and
12.2
,
K
has two non-adjacent neighbors in
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
and
K^{\prime}
has two non-adjacent neighbors
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
. Let
v\in K
. If
b
has a neighbor
v^{\prime}\in K^{\prime}
, then
v^{\prime}-b-v-a-N_{B_{i-1}}(a)-q_{i}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
b
is anticomplete to
K^{\prime}
. This implies that
a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}
are pairwise distinct.
If
aa^{\prime}\in E(G)
, then
a^{\prime}-a-v-b-N_{B_{i-1}}(b)-q_{i}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
\{a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\}
is stable.
Let
u\in N_{B_{i-1}}(a)
and
u^{\prime}\in N_{B_{i-1}}(a^{\prime})
.
Then
u
is anticomplete to
\{a^{\prime},b,b^{\prime}\}
and
u^{\prime}
is anticomplete to
\{a,b,b^{\prime}\}
. Then
b-v-a-u-u^{\prime}-a^{\prime}-K^{\prime}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
∎

Lemma 12.4
.

Let
a,b
be two non-adjacent simplicial vertices of the set that consists of all vertices in
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
complete to
A_{2}(i-2)
.
Then both of
N(a)\setminus B_{i+2}
and
N(b)\setminus B_{i+2}
are cliques, and so
N_{B_{i+2}}(a)
and
N_{B_{i+2}}(b)
are two disjoint complete cliques of size larger than
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Proof.

Suppose for a contradiction that
s,s^{\prime}
are two non-adjacent vertices in
N(a)\setminus B_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
8.11
,

N(a)\setminus B_{i+2}\subseteq B_{i-1}\cup A_{2}(i-2)\cup B_{i-2}\cup A_{3}(i-2)
.
Suppose first that
s\in B_{i-1}\cup A_{2}(i-2)
.
Since
B_{i-1}\cup A_{2}(i-2)
is a clique by Lemma
12.1
, we may assume
s^{\prime}\in B_{i-2}\cup A_{3}(i-2)
.
Since
s^{\prime}
is not complete to
B_{i-1}\cup A_{2}(i-2)
, we have
s^{\prime}b\notin E(G)
.
If
s^{\prime}
is complete to
A_{2}(i-2)
, then
\{s^{\prime},b\}
is also a special pair for
A_{2}(i-2)
and then
s^{\prime}
is complete to
A_{2}(i-2)\cup B_{i-1}
, a contradiction.
So
s^{\prime}
has a non-neighbor
s^{\prime\prime}\in A_{2}(i-2)
.
Then
s^{\prime}-a-s^{\prime\prime}-b-N_{B_{i+2}}(b)-q_{i+1}-q_{i}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
So we may assume that
s,s^{\prime}\in B_{i-2}\cup A_{3}(i-2)
.
By the assumption on
a
, we have at least one of
s,s^{\prime}
is not complete to
A_{2}(i-2)
.
By symmetry, we may assume that
s
has a non-neighbor
s^{\prime\prime}\in A_{2}(i-2)
.
Then
sb\notin E(G)
, and so
s-a-s^{\prime\prime}-b-N_{B_{i+2}}(b)-q_{i+1}-q_{i}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
∎

Lemma 12.5
.

There exists an index
i
such that
A_{2}=A_{2}(i-2)\cup A_{2}(i+1)
. Moreover, if
A_{2}(i-2)
and
A_{2}(i+1)
are not empty, then
A_{2}(i-2)
has two non-adjacent neighbors
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
complete to
A_{2}(i-2)
and
A_{2}(i+1)
has two non-adjacent neighbors
a,b\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i+2)
complete to
A_{2}(i+1)
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
A_{2}(i+2)\neq\emptyset
. By Lemme
10.1
, we may assume that a special pair
\{a,b\}
for
A_{2}(i+2)
are in
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
. If
v\in A_{2}(i+1)
, then
a-A_{2}(i+2)-b-N_{B_{i-1}}(b)-q_{i}-N_{B_{i+1}}(v)-v
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
A_{2}(i+1)=\emptyset
.

Suppose that
A_{2}(i)\neq\emptyset
. Let
\{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\}
be a special pair for
A_{2}(i)
.
Suppose first that
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i)
.
If
b,b^{\prime}
have a common neighbor
d\in B_{i-1}
, then
a-A_{2}(i+2)-b-d-b^{\prime}-A_{2}(i)-a^{\prime}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
So
b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}
have pairwise disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-1}
.
Then
a-A_{2}(i+2)-b-N_{B_{i-1}}(b)-N_{B_{i-1}}(b^{\prime})-b^{\prime}-A_{2}(i)
is an induced
P_{7}
.
So
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in A_{3}^{\prime}(i+1)
. By Lemma
12.1
,
B_{i-2}
is complete to
B_{i-1}
. It follows that
a
is complete to
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
and so
a,b
have a common neighbor in both
B_{i-1}
and
B_{i+2}
, a contradiction. So
A_{2}(i)=\emptyset
.

Now suppose that
A_{2}(i-2)\neq\emptyset
. By symmetry and
A_{2}(i+2)\neq\emptyset
, a special pair
\{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\}
for
A_{2}(i-2)
is contained in
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
. By Lemma
12.1
,
b^{\prime}
is complete to
B_{i-1}
and
b
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. If
b=b^{\prime}
, then
b
is complete to
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
and so
a,b
have a common neighbor in both
B_{i-1}
and
B_{i+2}
. So
b\neq b^{\prime}
. If
bb^{\prime}\in E(G)
, then one of
b
and
b^{\prime}
is complete to
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
by Lemma
8.2
. So
bb^{\prime}\notin E(G)
. Since
b
and
b^{\prime}
have a common neighbor in both
B_{i-1}
and
B_{i+2}
, it contradicts Lemma
8.2
. So
A_{2}(i-2)=\emptyset
.

This implies that if
A_{2}(i-1)\neq\emptyset
, then a special pair for
A_{2}(i-1)
is contained in
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-1)
. It follows that
i+1
is such an index.
∎

Lemma 12.6
.

There are at most one non-empty
A_{2}(j)
.

Proof.

Suppose not. By Lemma
12.5
, we may assume that
A_{2}=A_{2}(i-2)\cup A_{2}(i+1)
where
A_{2}(i-2)
and
A_{2}(i+1)
are not empty.

Let
a_{1},b_{1}
be two non-adjacent simplicial vertices of the set that consists of all vertices in
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
that complete to
A_{2}(i-2)
and
a_{2},b_{2}
be two non-adjacent simplicial vertices of the set that consists of all vertices in
A^{\prime}_{3}(i+2)
that complete to
A_{2}(i+1)
.
By symmetry, we may assume that
b_{1}\in A_{3}(i-2)
and
b_{2}\in A_{3}(i+2)
.
By Lemmas
8.13
and
12.4
,
N_{B_{i+2}}(a_{1})\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(b_{1})\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(a_{2})\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(b_{2})
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction.
∎

12.1
One non-empty
A_{2}(i)

Suppose that
A_{2}=A_{2}(i+2)\neq\emptyset
.
Let
\{a,b\}\subseteq A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
be a special pair for
A_{2}(i+2)
. By symmetry, we may assume that
b\in A_{3}(i-2)
.

Lemma 12.7
.

A_{3}(i)=A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
.

Proof.

Suppose that
A_{3}(i)\neq\emptyset
.
By Lemma
12.1
,
a,b
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-1}
and
B_{i}
is complete to
B_{i+1}
. But this contradicts Lemma
8.9
.

Suppose that
A_{3}(i+2)\neq\emptyset
. Then
B_{i+2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+2))\neq\emptyset
by Lemma
4.13
. By Lemma
8.11
,
B_{i+2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+2))
is anticomplete to
b
, which contradicts Lemma
12.1
.
∎

Lemma 12.8
.

|B_{i}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Proof.

Let
w\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1))
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i+2}
. By Lemma
12.7
,
N(w)\setminus B_{i}\subseteq B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
.
Suppose that
N(w)\setminus B_{i}
has two non-adjacent vertices
s,s^{\prime}

with
s\in B_{i+1}
and
s^{\prime}\in B_{i+2}
.
If
s\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1))
, then by the minimality of
w
, we have
s
has a neighbor
s^{\prime\prime}\in B_{i+2}
with
s^{\prime\prime}w\notin E(G)
.
Then
s-w-s^{\prime}-s^{\prime\prime}-s
is an induced
C_{4}
.
So we may assume that
s\in N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))
.
Then
N_{A_{3}(i+1)}(s)-s-w-s^{\prime}-a-N_{B_{i-1}}(a)-N_{B_{i-1}}(b)
is an induced
P_{7}
.
∎

Lemma 12.9
.

For every special pair
\{a_{0},b_{0}\}
of
A_{2}(i+2)
, each vertex in
(N(a_{0})\cup N(b_{0}))\cap A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
is complete to
A_{2}(i+2)
.

Proof.

Suppose not.
By symmetry, we may assume
a_{0}
has a neighbor
a^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
that has a non-neighbor
a^{\prime\prime}\in A_{2}(i+2)
.
By definition of
a_{0},b_{0}
and
C_{4}
-freeness of
G
,
a^{\prime}b_{0}\notin E(G)
.
Then
a^{\prime}-a_{0}-a^{\prime\prime}-b_{0}-N_{B_{i-1}}(b_{0})-q_{i}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
∎

Let
w
be a vertex in
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
with minimal neighborhood in
H
among all vertices in
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
.

Lemma 12.10
.

N(w)\setminus B_{i-1}
is a clique and so
|N_{B_{i-1}}(w)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Proof.

Suppose not.
Let
s,s^{\prime}
be two non-adjacent vertices in
N(w)\setminus B_{i-1}
.
By Lemmas
12.7
and
8.11
,
N(w)\setminus B_{i-1}\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)
.
Since
B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)
is a clique, we may assume that
s\in B_{i-2}
and
s^{\prime}\in B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)
.
If
s^{\prime}\in A_{2}(i+2)
, then
s-w-s^{\prime}-b-N_{B_{i-1}}(b)-q_{i}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
So we may assume that
s^{\prime}\in B_{i+2}
.
Suppose first that
s\in B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
.
By the minimality of
w
,
s
has a neighbor
s^{\prime\prime}
in
B_{i+2}\cup B_{i-1}
that is not adjacent to
w
.
If
s^{\prime\prime}\in B_{i+2}
, then
s^{\prime\prime}-s-w-s^{\prime}-s^{\prime\prime}
is an induced
C_{4}
.
If
s^{\prime\prime}\in B_{i-1}
, then
s^{\prime\prime}-s-w-s^{\prime}
is a
P_{4}
-structure.
So we may assume that
s
has a neighbor
s^{\prime\prime}\in A_{3}(i-2)
.
Since
s^{\prime\prime}-s-w-s^{\prime}-q_{i+1}-q_{i}-B_{i-1}
is not a bad
P_{7}
, we have
w
is complete to
B_{i-1}
.
So
w
and
s^{\prime\prime}
have a common neighbor in
B_{i-1}
, and hence
w,s^{\prime\prime}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
8.1
(2),
w-s^{\prime}-N_{B_{i+2}}(s^{\prime\prime})-s-w
is an induced
C_{4}
.
∎

By Lemma
2.4
, we can assume that
a,b
are simplicial vertices of the set that consists of all vertices in
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
complete to
A_{2}(i+2)
.

Lemma 12.11
.

a\in B_{i-2}
.

Proof.

Suppose not.
By Lemmas
12.10
and
12.4
,
|N_{B_{i-1}}(w)|,|N_{B_{i-1}}(a)|,|N_{B_{i-1}}(b)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Since
a,b,w
are pairwise non-adjacent and they have a common neighbor in
B_{i+2}
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(w)
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(a)
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(b)
are pairwise disjoint. By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(w)
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(a)
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(b)
are complete to
B_{i}
. By Lemma
12.8
,

N_{B_{i-1}}(w)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(a)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(b)\cup B_{i}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 12.12
.

Let
v
be a
(A_{3}(i-2),B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2))
-minimal simplicial vertex.
Then
N(v)\setminus B_{i-1}
is a clique and so
|N_{B_{i-1}}(v)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Proof.

Suppose not.
Let
s,s^{\prime}
be two non-adjacent neighbors of
v
in
N(v)\setminus B_{i-1}
.
Note that
N(v)\setminus B_{i-1}\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)\cup A_{3}(i-2)
.
Since
v
is simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
and by Lemma
8.3
, we have at most one of
s,s^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
.
Since
B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)
is a clique, we may assume that
s\in A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
and
s^{\prime}\in B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)
.
Assume first that
s\in B_{i-2}
.
By Lemma
8.1
(2),
s^{\prime}\in A_{2}(i+2)
.
By Lemma
12.11
,
as\in E(G)
and so
sb\notin E(G)
by
G
is
C_{4}
-free.
Then
s-a-s^{\prime}-b-N_{B_{i-1}}(b)-q_{i}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
So we may assume that
s\in A_{3}(i-2)
.
If
s
is simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
, then
s
has a neighbor
s^{\prime\prime}\in A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i+2}
that is not adjacent to
v
by the minimality of
v
, and so
s-s^{\prime\prime}-s^{\prime}-v-s
is an induced
C_{4}
.
So
s
is not simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
and thus
s
has a neighbor
s^{\prime\prime}\in A_{3}(i-2)
that is not adjacent to
v
.
It follows that
v-s-s^{\prime\prime}
is an induced
P_{3}
in
A_{3}(i-2)
.
By Lemma
8.4
,
s^{\prime}\in A_{2}(i+2)
.
Since
s
is not complete to
A_{2}(i+2)
,
v\neq a,b
by Lemma
12.9
. If
v
is complete to
A_{2}(i+2)
, then
v
is adjacent to
a
and
b
by Lemma
12.9
. Then
a\in B_{i-2}
mixes on
vb\in A_{3}(i-2)
, which contradicts Lemma
8.3
. So
v
is not complete to
A_{2}(i+2)
and so
av\notin E(G)
or
bv\notin E(G)
by Lemma
12.1
.
By symmetry, we assume that
av\notin E(G)
. Then
s-v-s^{\prime}-a-N_{B_{i-1}}(a)-q_{i}-q_{i+1}
is an induced
P_{7}
.
∎

Lemma 12.13
.

A_{3}(i-2)\cup A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique.

Proof.

We first show that
A_{3}(i-2)
is a clique.
Suppose not.
By Lemma
2.6
, there are two non-adjacent
(A_{3}(i-2),A_{2}(i+2)\cup B_{i+2})
-minimal simplicial vertices
v_{1},v_{2}
.
If
b
is adjacent to both
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
, then
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
complete to
A_{2}(i+2)
by Lemma
12.9
, which contradicts the choice of
b
.
So we may assume that
v_{1}
is not adjacent to
b
.
For convenience, let
v=v_{1}
.
Suppose first that
av\notin E(G)
. Since
a,b,v
have a common neighbor in
B_{i+2}
,
a,b,v
have pairwise disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-1}
. By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(v)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(a)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(b)\cup B_{i}
is a clique.
By Lemmas
12.12
,
12.4
and
12.8
,
|N_{B_{i-1}}(v)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(a)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(b)\cup B_{i}|>\omega
, a contradiction.
So
av\in E(G)
. By Lemma
12.9
,
v
is complete to
A_{2}(i+2)
and so
\{v,b\}
is a special pair for
A_{2}(i+2)
. By Lemma
8.3
,
v
and
b
are simplicial in
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)
. The fact that
v,b\in A_{3}(i-2)
contradicts Lemma
12.11
. This proves that
A_{3}(i-2)
is a clique.

By Lemma
12.9
,
A_{3}(i-2)
is complete to
A_{2}(i+2)
. Since
a\in B_{i-2}
,
a
is anticomplete to
A_{3}(i-2)
by Lemma
8.3
. For any vertex
v\in A_{3}(i-2)
,
\{a,v\}
is a special pair for
A_{2}(i+2)
and so
v
is complete to
B_{i+2}
by Lemma
12.1
. This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 12.14
.

B_{i-2}
is complete to
B_{i+2}\cup A_{2}(i+2)
.

Proof.

Since
a\in B_{i-2}
,
B_{i-2}
is complete to
A_{2}(i+2)
by Lemma
12.9
. By Lemma
4.8
,
B_{i-2}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
.
∎

Lemma 12.15
.

A_{3}(i-1)=\emptyset
.

Proof.

Suppose not.
Let
u
be a
(A_{3}(i-1),H)
-minimal simplicial vertex.
By Lemma
8.14
,
|N_{B_{i}}(u)|,|N_{B_{i-2}}(u)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Let
w^{\prime}\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-1))
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
H
.
By Lemma
8.15
,
|N_{B_{i}}(w^{\prime})|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and
|N_{B_{i-2}}(w^{\prime})|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
If
N_{B_{i}}(w^{\prime})
and
N_{B_{i}}(u)
are disjoint, then
|B_{i}|>2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and so
N_{B_{i-1}}(w)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(b)\cup B_{i}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
by Lemmas
12.10
and
12.4
(recall that
w
is the vertex defined before Lemma
12.10
).

So we may assume that
N_{B_{i}}(w^{\prime})
and
N_{B_{i}}(u)
have a common vertex and so
N_{B_{i-2}}(w^{\prime})
and
N_{B_{i-2}}(u)
are disjoint.
By Lemma
8.13
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(b)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(w)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(u)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(w^{\prime})
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 12.16
.

A_{3}(i+1)
is a clique.

Proof.

Suppose not.
By Lemma
2.6
, there are two non-adjacent
(A_{3}(i+1),H)
-minimal simplicial vertices
v_{1},v_{2}
.
By Lemma
8.14
,
|N_{B_{i}}(v_{1})|,|N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{1})|,|N_{B_{i}}(v_{2})|,|N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{2})|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
If
N_{B_{i}}(v_{1})
and
N_{B_{i}}(v_{2})
are disjoint, then
N_{B_{i}}(v_{1})\cup N_{B_{i}}(v_{2})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(a)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(b)
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
by Lemmas
12.4
and
8.10
.
So we may assume that
N_{B_{i}}(v_{1})
and
N_{B_{i}}(v_{2})
have a common vertex and so
N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{1})
and
N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{2})
are disjoint.
Let
w^{\prime}
be a vertex in
B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1))
that has minimal neighborhood in
H
.
By Lemma
8.15
,
|N_{B_{i+2}}(w^{\prime})|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. Since
B_{i}
is complete to
B_{i+1}
, each of
w^{\prime},v_{1}
and
w^{\prime},v_{2}
have a common neighbor in
B_{i}
. So
N_{B_{i+2}}(w^{\prime})
,
N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{1})
and
N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{2})
are pairwise disjoint cliques that are complete to
A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2))
by Lemma
8.10
.

Let
v
be a vertex in
B_{i-1}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i-2}
.
Since
A_{3}(i-2)\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique by Lemma
12.13
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i-2))
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
.
It follows that
N(v)\setminus(A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2)))\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i-1}
.
By the minimality of
v
,
N(v)\setminus(A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2)))
is a clique.
So
|A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2))|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Hence,
(A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2)))\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(w^{\prime})\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{1})\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{2})
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction.
∎

Next, we collect some information about the size of certain sets.

Lemma 12.17
.

The following statements hold

(1)

|A_{3}(i+1)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))|,|A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2))|,|N_{B_{i+2}}(A_{3}(i+1))|,|B_{i+2}\cap N(B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1)))|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

(2)

|B_{i-1}|,|B_{i+1}|,|A_{3}(i+1)\cup(B_{i+1}\cap N(A_{3}(i+1)))|<\frac{3}{4}\omega
.

(3)

|A_{3}(i-2)\cup(B_{i-2}\cap N(A_{3}(i-2)))|,|B_{i}|,|B_{i+1}\cap N(A_{3}(i+1))|<\frac{\omega}{2}
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
8.15
and
8.14
,
|N_{B_{i+2}}(A_{3}(i+1))|
,
|B_{i+2}\cap N(B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1)))|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Let
u\in B_{i-1}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i-2}
.
Since
N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i-2))
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
,
u
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
.
By the minimality of
u
,
N(u)\setminus(A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2)))\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i-1}
is a clique and so
|N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2))\cup A_{3}(i-2)|\geq|N(u)\cap(N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2))\cup A_{3}(i-2))|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Moreover,
N(u)\cap(N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2))\cup A_{3}(i-2))
is complete to
B_{i-1}
and so
|B_{i-1}|<\frac{3}{4}\omega
.

Let
v\in B_{i+2}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i-2}
.
Since
N_{B_{i+2}}(A_{3}(i+1))
is anticomplete to
B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1))
,
v
is anticomplete to
B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1))
.
By the minimality of
v
,
N(v)\setminus(A_{3}(i+1)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1)))\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i-1}
is a clique and so
|A_{3}(i+1)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Let
w\in A_{3}(i+1)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+2}
.
Since
N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))
is complete to
N_{B_{i+2}}(B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1)))
,
w\in A_{3}(i+1)
.
Since
A_{3}(i+1)
is a clique by Lemma
12.16
,
w
is simplicial in
A_{3}(i+1)
and so
|N_{B_{i+2}}(w)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
by Lemma
8.14
.
By the minimality of
w
,
N_{B_{i+2}}(w)
is complete to
A_{3}(i+1)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))
and so
|A_{3}(i+1)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))|<\frac{3}{4}\omega
.

Let
x\in B_{i+1}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+2}
.
Since
N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))
is complete to
N_{B_{i+2}}(A_{3}(i+1))
,
x\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1))
.
By Lemma
8.15
,
|N_{B_{i+2}}(x)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and so by the minimality of
x
,
N_{B_{i+2}}(x)
is complete to
B_{i+1}
.
It follows that
|B_{i+1}|<\frac{3}{4}\omega
.

Since
|B_{i+2}|>2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, and
A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2))
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))
are complete to
B_{i+2}
,
|A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2))|
and
|N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))|<\frac{\omega}{2}
.
Since
B_{i}
is complete to two disjoint cliques of size larger than
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
in
B_{i-1}
by Lemma
8.10
,
8.15
and
8.14
,
|B_{i}|<\frac{\omega}{2}
.
∎

We are now ready to give a desired coloring of
G
by considering whether
A_{3}(i+1)
is empty or not.

Lemma 12.18
.

If
A_{3}(i+1)\neq\emptyset
,
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=|A_{3}(i-2)\cup(B_{i-2}\cap N(A_{3}(i-2)))|,

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=|B_{i+2}\cap N(B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1)))|,

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle=|B_{i+2}\cap N(A_{3}(i+1))|.

By Lemma
12.14
,
x+y+z\leq\omega
.
Our strategy is to use
p=x+y+z-3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
colors to color some vertices of
G
first and then argue that the remaining vertices can be colored with
f=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-p
colors.
Note that

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-(x+y+z-3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil)

\displaystyle=4\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil+(\omega-(x+y+z))

\displaystyle\geq\omega,

since
x+y+z\leq\omega
.

Step 1: Precolor a subgraph.

Next we define vertices to be colored with
p
colors. Let
S_{i}\subseteq B_{i}
be the set of
p
vertices with largest neighborhoods in
H
,
S_{i-2}\subseteq A_{3}(i-2)\cup(B_{i-2}\cap N(A_{3}(i-2)))
be the set of
x-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices with largest neighborhoods in
H
,
S^{y}_{i+2}\subseteq B_{i+2}\cap N(B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1)))
be the set of
y-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices with largest neighborhoods in
H
, and

S^{z}_{i+2}\subseteq B_{i+2}\cap N(A_{3}(i+1))
be the set of
z-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices with largest neighborhoods in
H
.
Since
(x+y+z)-3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil<|B_{i}|
, the choice of
S_{i}
is possible. We color
S_{i}
with all colors in
[p]
, and color all vertices in
S_{i-2}\cup S^{y}_{i+2}\cup S^{z}_{i+2}
with all colors in
[p]
. Since
B_{i}
is anticomplete to
A^{\prime}_{3}(i-2)\cup B_{i+2}
, this coloring is proper. So it suffices to show that

G_{1}=G-(S_{i}\cup S_{i-2}\cup S^{y}_{i+2}\cup S^{z}_{i+2})
is
f
-colorable.

Step 2: Reduce by degeneracy.

Next, we use Lemma
11.69
to further reduce the problem to color a subgraph of
G_{1}
. Let
R_{i-1}
be the set of vertices in
B_{i-1}
that is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
. Let
v\in R_{i-1}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
among
R_{i-1}
. Since
v
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
,
v
is minimal among
B_{i-1}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=\left(B_{i},B_{i-1},\left(A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}\left(A_{3}(i-2)\right)\right)\setminus S_{i-2},A_{2}(i-1)\right)
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{1}-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable.

Let
R^{y}_{i+1}=B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1))
. Let
v\in R^{y}_{i+1}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i+2}\cup B_{i}
among
R^{y}_{i+1}
. Since
v
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+2}}(A_{3}(i+1))
,
v
is minimal among
B_{i+1}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=\left(B_{i},B_{i+1},N_{B_{i+2}}(R^{y}_{i+1})\setminus S^{y}_{i+2},A_{2}(i)\right)
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}-R_{i-1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}-R_{i-1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
(G_{1}-R_{i-1})-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
(G_{1}-R_{i-1})-R^{y}_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable.

Let
R^{z}_{i+1}=A_{3}(i+1)
. Let
v\in R^{z}_{i+1}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i+2}\cup B_{i}
among
R^{z}_{i+1}
. Since
v
is anticomplete to
B_{i+2}\setminus N_{B_{i+2}}(R^{z}_{i+1})
,
v
is minimal among
N_{B_{i+1}}(R^{z}_{i+1})\cup R^{z}_{i+1}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=\left(B_{i},R^{z}_{i+1}\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(R^{z}_{i+1}),N_{B_{i+2}}(R^{z}_{i+1})\setminus S^{z}_{i+2},A_{2}(i)\right)
,
P=S_{i}
, and
F=G_{1}-R_{i-1}-R^{y}_{i+1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}-R_{i-1}-R^{y}_{i+1}}(v)<f
. It follows that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}-R^{y}_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
(G_{1}-R_{i-1}-R^{y}_{i+1})-v
is
f
-colorable. Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
we conclude that
G_{1}-R_{i-1}-R^{y}_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable if only if
G_{1}-R_{i-1}-R^{y}_{i+1}-R^{z}_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable.

Step 3: Reduce simplicial vertices.

Now it suffices to show that
G_{2}=G_{1}-R_{i-1}-R^{y}_{i+1}-R^{z}_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable. Note that every vertex in
A_{3}(i-2)
is simplicial in
G_{2}
.
So
G_{2}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{2}-A_{3}(i-2)
is
f
-colorable.
Since every vertex in
A_{2}(i+2)
is simplicial in
G_{2}-A_{3}(i-2)
,
G_{2}-A_{3}(i-2)
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{3}=G_{2}-A_{3}(i-2)-A_{2}(i+2)
is
f
-colorble.

Step 4: Color
G_{3}
.

So it suffices to show that
G_{3}
is
f
-colorable. Let
H^{\prime}
be a subgraph of
G_{3}
which is hyperhole with maximum chromatic number. It follows that
\omega(H^{\prime})\leq\omega\leq f
.
It is shown in
[
14
]
(Theorem 2.1) that
\chi(G_{3})=\chi(H^{\prime})
.
If
H^{\prime}
is perfect, then
\chi(H^{\prime})=\omega(H^{\prime})\leq f
.
So
H^{\prime}\cap B_{j}
is not empty for
j\in[5]
.
Let
M_{1}=(H^{\prime}\cap B_{i-1})\cup(H^{\prime}\cap B_{i})
.
By the definition of
S_{i}
,
M_{1}\cup S_{i}
is a clique of
G
.
By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i-2))
is complete to
B_{i}
and so
(M_{1}\cup S_{i})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i-2))
is also a clique of
G
.
Then
|M_{1}|\leq\omega-|S_{i}|-|N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i-2))|\leq\omega-p-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Let
M_{2}=(H^{\prime}\cap B_{i-2})\cup(H^{\prime}\cap B_{i+2})
.
By Lemma
8.10
,
S_{i+2}^{y}
and
S_{i+2}^{z}
are complete to
B_{i-2}
and so
M_{2}\cup S_{i+2}^{y}\cup S_{i+2}^{z}
is a clique of
G
.
Then
|M_{2}|\leq\omega-|S_{i+2}^{y}|-|S_{i+2}^{z}|\leq\omega-(y+z)+2\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Let
M_{3}=H^{\prime}\cap B_{i+1}
. Note that
M_{3}\subseteq N_{B_{i+1}}(A_{3}(i+1))
.
By Lemma
8.1
(3),
M_{3}
is complete to
N_{B_{i+2}}(A_{3}(i+1))
and
N_{B_{i+2}}(B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1)))

and so
|M_{3}|<\frac{\omega}{2}
.
Then

\displaystyle|V(H^{\prime})|=
