\displaystyle|M_{1}|+|M_{2}|+|M_{3}|

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle(\omega-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-(x+y+z)+3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil)+(\omega-(y+z)+2\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil)+\frac{\omega}{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\omega+4\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil+(x+\frac{\omega}{2})-2(x+y+z)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(\omega+4\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-(x+y+z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2f,

where the last inequality is due to
x\leq\frac{\omega}{2}
.
It follows that
\chi(G_{3})=\chi(H^{\prime})=\max\left\{\omega(H^{\prime}),\left\lceil\frac{|V(H^{\prime})|}{2}\right\rceil\right\}\leq f
.
∎

Lemma 12.19
.

If
A_{3}(i+1)=\emptyset
, then
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=|B_{i}|,

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=|N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i-2))|.

By Lemma
8.10
,
x+y<\omega-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\leq\frac{3}{4}\omega
.
Our strategy is to use
p=x+y-2\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
colors to color some vertices of
G
first and then argue that the remaining vertices can be colored with
f=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-p
colors.
Note that

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-(x+y-2\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil)

\displaystyle=\omega+(3\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-(x+y))

\displaystyle\geq\omega,

since
x+y\leq\frac{3}{4}\omega
.

Step 1: Precolor a subgraph
Next we define vertices to be colored with colors in
[p]
.
Let
S_{i}\subseteq B_{i}
with
|S_{i}|=x-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
,
S_{i-1}\subseteq N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i-2))
with
|S_{i-1}|=y-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, and
S_{i+2}\subseteq B_{i+2}
be the set of
p
vertices with largest neighborhoods in
H
.
Since
p=x+y-2\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil<\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\leq|B_{i+2}|
, the choice of
S_{i+2}
is possible.
We color all vertices in
S_{i}\cup S_{i-1}
with all colors in
[p]
and color
S_{i+2}
with all colors in
[p]
.
Since
B_{i+2}
is anticomplete to
B_{i}\cup B_{i-1}
, this coloring is proper.
So it suffices to show that
G_{1}=G-(S_{i}\cup S_{i-1}\cup S_{i+2})
is
f
-colorable.

Step 2: Reduce by degeneracy
Next we use Lemma
11.69
to reduce the problem to color a subgraph of
G_{1}
.

Let
v\in A_{3}(i-2)
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=\left(B_{i+2},A_{3}(i-2)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(A_{3}(i-2)),N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i-2))\setminus S_{i-1},A_{2}(i+2)\right)
,
P=S_{i+2}
, and
F=G_{1}
, we have
d_{G_{1}}(v)<f
.
It follows that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{1}-v
is
f
-colorable.
Repeatedly applying Lemma
11.69
, we conclude that
G_{1}
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{1}-A_{3}(i-2)
is
f
-colorable.

Let
v\in B_{i+1}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
11.69
with
(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X^{*})=\left(B_{i+2},B_{i+1},B_{i}\setminus S_{i},\emptyset\right)
,
P=S_{i+2}
, and
F=G_{1}-A_{3}(i-2)
, we have
d_{G_{1}-A_{3}(i-2)}(v)<f
.
It follows that
G_{1}-A_{3}(i-2)
is
f
-colorable if and only if
(G_{1}-A_{3}(i-2))-v
is
f
-colorable.
Repeatedly applying Lemma 11.19, we conclude that
G_{1}-A_{3}(i-2)
is
f
-colorable if and only if
G_{2}=G_{1}-A_{3}(i-2)-B_{i+1}
is
f
-colorable.

Since
G_{2}
is chordal,
\chi(G_{2})\leq\omega(G_{2})\leq\omega\leq f
.
∎

13
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
are empty

Now we can assume that
A_{1}=A_{2}=\emptyset
. Since
A_{5}
is universal,
A_{5}=\emptyset
. So
G=H\cup A_{3}
.

Lemma 13.1
.

One of the following holds.

(1)

A_{3}=A_{3}(i-1)\cup A_{3}(i+1)
for some
i\in[5]
where
A_{3}(i-1)
and
A_{3}(i+1)
are non-empty cliques, and for every
v\in A_{3}(i-1)
and
u\in A_{3}(i+1)
,
N_{B_{i}}(v)
is anticomplete to
B_{i-1}\setminus N(v)
and
N_{B_{i+2}}(u)
is anticomplete to
B_{i+1}\setminus N(u)
.

(2)

A_{3}=A_{3}(i-2)\cup A_{3}(i+2)
for some
i\in[5]
where
A_{3}(i-2)
and
A_{3}(i+2)
are non-empty cliques, and for every
v\in A_{3}(i-2)
and
u\in A_{3}(i+2)
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(v)
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(v)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(u)
is anticomplete to
B_{i+2}\setminus N(u)
.

(3)

A_{3}=A_{3}(i-2)
for some
i\in[5]
and
A_{3}(i-2)
is a blowup of
P_{3}
.

Proof.

We start with the following property.

(i) Let
v
be a simplicial vertex in
A_{3}(i-2)
such that
N_{B_{i+2}}(v)
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(v)
.
Then
A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
.

Suppose to the contrary that
A_{3}(i+2)\neq\emptyset
.
Let
w\in B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
8.15
and Lemma
8.14
, we have
N_{B_{i+2}}(v)
and
N_{B_{i+2}}(w)
are disjoint cliques larger than
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

Let
u
be a simplicial vertex in
A_{3}(i+2)
and
w^{\prime}\in B_{i+2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+2))
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i-2}
.
By Lemma
4.10
,
B_{i+2}\setminus N(u)
is anticomplete to either
N_{B_{i+1}}(u)
or
N_{B_{i-2}}(u)
.
Suppose first that
B_{i+2}\setminus N(u)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(u)
.
Then
N_{B_{i+2}}(v)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(w)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(u)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(w^{\prime})
is a clique larger than
\omega
by Lemma
8.10
, Lemma
8.15
and Lemma
8.14
, a contradiction.
So we may assume that
B_{i+2}\setminus N(u)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i-2}}(u)
.
Then
N_{B_{i+2}}(v)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(w)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(u)\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(w^{\prime})
is a clique larger than
\omega
by Lemma
8.12
, Lemma
8.15
and Lemma
8.14
, a contradiction.
This completes the proof of (i).

Since
A_{3}(i)\neq\emptyset
, we may assume by symmetry that
A_{3}(i-2)\neq\emptyset
.
Let
v_{i-2}\in A_{3}(i-2)
be a vertex simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
. By symmetry and Lemma
4.10
, we may assume that
N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{i-2})
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(v_{i-2})
. By (1), we have
A_{3}(i+2)=\emptyset
.

(ii) If
A_{3}(i+1)\neq\emptyset
, then (1) holds.

By Lemma
8.9
, we have
N_{B_{i}}(u)
is anticomplete to
B_{i+1}\setminus N(u)
for every
u\in A_{3}(i+1)
.
By (i) and Lemma
8.9
,
A_{3}(i)=A_{3}(i-1)=\emptyset
and so
A_{3}=A_{3}(i-2)\cup A_{3}(i+1)
.

Suppose first that
A_{3}(i+1)
is not a clique.
Then
A_{3}(i+1)
has two non-adjacent simplicial vertices
u_{1}^{\prime},u_{2}^{\prime}
.
For
j\in[2]
, let
u_{j}\in N_{A_{3}(i+1)}[u^{\prime}_{j}]
be a vertex simplicial in
A_{3}(i+1)
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
2.6
,
u_{1}u_{2}\notin E(G)
.
By Lemma
8.9
, we have
u_{1},u_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
.
Let
w
be a vertex in
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
and
w^{\prime}
be a vertex in
B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i+1))
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+2}
.
By Lemmas
8.10
,
8.15
and
8.14
, we have
N_{B_{i-1}}(w)\cup N_{B_{i}}(w^{\prime})\cup N_{B_{i}}(u_{1})\cup N_{B_{i}}(u_{2})
is a clique larger than
\omega
.
So
A_{3}(i+1)
is a clique. Let
K=A_{3}(i+1)
.

Suppose that
A_{3}(i-2)
is not a clique.
Then
A_{3}(i-2)
has two non-adjacent simplicial vertices
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}
.
For
j\in[2]
, let
v_{j}\in N_{A_{3}(i-2)}[v^{\prime}_{j}]
be a vertex simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
2.6
,
v_{1}v_{2}\notin E(G)
.
Let
w\in B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
.
If any two of
w,v_{1},v_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-1}
, then
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
has a clique larger than
\omega
by Lemmas
8.10
,
8.15
and
8.14
, a contradiction.
So we may assume that
w,v_{1},v_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i+2}
.
Let
u\in B_{i}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+1}
.
Note that
N(u)\setminus(K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K))\subseteq B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
.
Then by the minimality of
u
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(u)\cup N_{B_{i}}(u)
is a clique and so
K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
is a clique of size larger than
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
by Lemma
8.3
.
Then
N_{B_{i+2}}(w)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{1})\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{2})\cup K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
is a clique larger than
\omega
by Lemmas
8.10
,
8.15
and
8.14
, a contradiction.

Since
A_{3}(i-2)
is a clique, every
v\in A_{3}(i-2)
and
v_{i-2}
have a common neighbor in
B_{i+2}
.
If
N_{B_{i+2}}(v)
is not anticomplete to
B_{i-1}\setminus N(v)
, then
v
and
w
have a common neighbor in
B_{i+2}
and so
N_{H}(v_{i-2})\subsetneq N_{H}(v)
.
Since
v_{i-2}
and
w
have a common neighbor in
B_{i-1}
,
v
and
w
have a common neighbor in
B_{i-1}
, contradicts
G
is
C_{4}
-free.
So
N_{B_{i+2}}(v)
is anticomplete to
B_{i+2}\setminus N(v)
.
This completes the proof of (ii).

So we may assume that
A_{3}(i+1)=\emptyset
.

(iii) If
A_{3}(i)\neq\emptyset
, then (1) holds.

Let
v
be a simplicial vertex of
A_{3}(i)
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+1}
.
If
B_{i}\setminus N(v)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(v)
, then we are done by (2).
So we may assume that
B_{i}\setminus N(v)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(v)
.
By Lemmas
8.10
,
8.15
and
8.14
, we have
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
has a clique larger than
\omega
, a contradiction.
This completes the proof of (iii).

So we may assume that
A_{3}(i)=\emptyset
.

(iv) If
A_{3}(i-1)\neq\emptyset
, then (2) holds.

Let
w
be a vertex in
B_{i-2}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-2))
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
and
w^{\prime}
be a vertex in
B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i-1))
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i-2}
.

Suppose first that
A_{3}(i-1)
is not a clique.
Then
A_{3}(i-1)
has two non-adjacent simplicial vertices
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}
.
For
j\in[2]
, let
v_{j}\in N_{A_{3}(i-1)}[v^{\prime}_{j}]
be a vertex simplicial in
A_{3}(i-1)
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i-2}
.
By Lemma
2.6
,
v_{1}v_{2}\notin E(G)
.
By (1), we have
v_{1},w^{\prime}
and
v_{2},w^{\prime}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
.
If
v_{1},v_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-2}
, then
N_{B_{i-2}}(v_{1})\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(v_{2})\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{i-2})\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(w)
is a clique larger than
\omega
by Lemmas
8.10
,
8.15
and
8.14
, a contradiction.
So we may assume that
v_{1},v_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i}
.
Then
N_{B_{i}}(w^{\prime})\cup N_{B_{i}}(v_{1})\cup N_{B_{i}}(v_{2})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(v_{i-2})
is a clique larger than
\omega
by Lemmas
8.1
(3),
8.11
,
8.15
and
8.14
, a contradiction.
So
A_{3}(i-1)
is a clique.

Let
v_{i-1}
be a simplicial vertex of
A_{3}(i-1)
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i}\cup B_{i-2}
.
By (1), we have
N_{B_{i}}(v_{i-1})
is anticomplete to
B_{i-1}\setminus N(v_{i-1})
.
Since
A_{3}(i-1)
is a clique, we have
u
and
v_{i-1}
have a common neighbor in
B_{i}
for every
u\in A_{3}(i-1)
. Let
u\in A_{3}(i-1)
.
If
N_{B_{i}}(u)
is not anticomplete to
B_{i-1}\setminus N(u)
, then
u
and
w^{\prime}
have a common neighbor in
B_{i}
and so
N_{H}(v_{i-1})\subseteq N_{H}(u)
.
Since
v_{i-1}
and
w^{\prime}
have a common neighbor in
B_{i-2}
,
u
and
w^{\prime}
have a common neighbor in
B_{i-2}
, which contradicts
G
is
C_{4}
-free.
By symmetry,
A_{3}(i-2)
is a clique and
N_{B_{i+2}}(v)
is anticomplete to
B_{i-2}\setminus N(v)
for every
v\in A_{3}(i-2)
.
This completes the proof of (iv).

So we may assume that
A_{3}=A_{3}(i-2)
.

(v) If
A_{3}=A_{3}(i-2)
, then
(3)
holds.

Let
u\in B_{i}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+1}
.
Since
N(u)\setminus B_{i+1}
is a clique, we have
|N_{B_{i+1}}(u)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and so
|B_{i+1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
By symmetry, we have
|B_{i}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

We first show that there are no three pairwise non-adjacent simplicial vertices of
A_{3}(i-2)
. Suppose for a contradiction that
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime},v_{3}^{\prime}
are three pairwise non-adjacent simplicial vertices of
A_{3}(i-2)
.
For
j\in[3]
, let
v_{j}\in N_{A_{3}(i-2)}[v^{\prime}_{j}]
be a vertex simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
.
By Lemma
2.6
,
v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}
are pairwise non-adjacent.
By Lemmas
8.10
,
8.15
and
8.14
, we have that no three of
w,v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}
have pairwise disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i+2}
or in
B_{i-1}
. In other word, no three of
w,v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}
have pairwise neighborhood containment in
B_{i+2}
or in
B_{i-1}
. So, we may assume that
v_{2}
and
v_{3}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i+2}
. Then
v_{1}
has neighborhood containment with either both
v_{2}
and
v_{3}
in
B_{i+2}
, or exactly one of
v_{2}
and
v_{3}
in
B_{i+2}
.

Suppose first that
v_{1}
has neighborhood containment with both
v_{2}
and
v_{3}
in
B_{i+2}
. As
w
has neighborhood containment with at least one of
v_{2}
and
v_{3}
in
B_{i+2}
,
w
and
v_{1}
have neighborhood containment in
B_{i+2}
. Then
w
,
v_{1}
and one of
v_{2}
and
v_{3}
have pairwise neighborhood containment in
B_{i+2}
, a contradiction. So, we may assume that
v_{1}
has neighborhood containment with exactly one of
v_{2}
and
v_{3}
in
B_{i+2}
.

By symmetry, we may assume that
v_{1}
has neighborhood containment with
v_{2}
in
B_{i+2}
. Then
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
have disjoint neighborhood in
B_{i-1}
and
v_{3}
have neighborhood containment with both
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
in
B_{i-1}
. By same argument, as
w
has neighborhood containment with at least one of
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
in
B_{i-1}
,
w
,
v_{3}
and one of
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
have pairwise neighborhood containment in
B_{i-1}
, a contradiction. Therefore, there are no three pairwise non-adjacent simplicial vertices of
A_{3}(i-2)
.

This immediately implies that
A_{3}(i-2)
has at most two components and if
A_{3}(i-2)
has two components, then
A_{3}(i-2)
is a disjoint union of two cliques. So we assume that
A_{3}(i-2)
is connected. By Lemma
8.5
,
A_{3}(i-2)
can be partitioned into two complete subsets
X
and
Y
. Since
G
is
C_{4}
-free, we may assume that
X
is a clique. Choose such a partition
(X,Y)
such that
|X|
is maximum. It follows that
Y
is disconnected. Since any
v\in Y
simplicial in
Y
is also simplicial in
A_{3}(i-2)
, it follows that
Y
has two component and each component is a clique. So
A_{3}(i)
is a blowup of
P_{3}
. This completes the proof of (v).

This completes the proof of Lemma
13.1
.
∎

Next we color
H\cup A_{3}
in terms of the outcomes of Lemma
13.1
. For a subset
S\subseteq B_{i}
and
v\in S
,
v
is
minimal
if
N_{H}(v)
is minimal over all vertices in
S
, and
v
is
maximal
if
N_{H}(v)
is maximal over all vertices in
S
.

13.1
Distance 2

In this subsection, we handle the first outcome of Lemma
13.1
.

Lemma 13.2
.

Let
G=H\cup K\cup J
where

K
is a non-empty component of
A_{3}(i-1)
and
J
is a non-empty component of
A_{3}(i+1)
.
Then
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

By Lemma
13.1
, both
K
and
J
are cliques. Moreover,
B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i}}(K)
and
B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+2}}(J)
. Let
z\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
and let
w\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)
. Then
N_{B_{i}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i}}(z)
are disjoint and
N_{B_{i+2}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+2}}(w)
are disjoint.

By Lemma
8.15
and Lemma
8.14
,
|B_{i-2}|
,
|N_{B_{i}}(K)|
,
|N_{B_{i}}(z)|
,
|N_{B_{i+2}}(J)|
,
|N_{B_{i+2}}(w)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. By Lemma
8.1
(4), there is a vertex in
N_{B_{i-2}}(K)
complete to
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
. We set this vertex to be
q_{i-2}
. Let
v_{K}\in K
that has maximum neighborhood in
H
. Let
v_{K}^{\prime\prime}\in K
that has minimal neighborhood in
H
and let
v_{J}^{\prime\prime}\in J
that has minimal neighborhood in
H
.

We set
F=(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3},F_{4},F_{5},O)
as follows:

•

F_{1}=\{q_{i-2}\}
,

•

F_{2}=\begin{cases}\{q_{i-1},q_{i-1}^{\prime},a\}\quad&\text{if }\,\,|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|\geq 2\\
\{q_{i-1},a,a^{\prime}\}\quad&\text{if }\,\,|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|=1,\end{cases}

where
a,a^{\prime}\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
are the first and second maximal in
B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
, respectively,

•

F_{3}=\{q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},b\}
, where
b\in N_{B_{i}}(v_{K}^{\prime\prime})
,

•

F_{4}=\{q_{i+1},c\}
, where
c\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)
is a maximal vertex in
B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)
,

•

F_{5}=\{q_{i+2},d,d^{\prime}\}
, where
d,d^{\prime}\in N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{J}^{\prime\prime})
,

•

O=\begin{cases}\{v_{K}\}\quad&\text{if }\,\,|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|\geq 2\\
\{v_{K},v_{K}^{\prime}\}\quad&\text{if }\,\,|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|=1,\end{cases}

where
v_{K}^{\prime}\in K\setminus\{v_{K}\}
that has maximal neighborhood in
H
.

We can assign color
1
to
a
,
b
and
d
, color
2
to
q_{i-1}
and
q_{i+1}
, color
3
to
v_{K}
,
q_{i}
and
q_{i+2}
, color
4
to
q_{i-2}
and
q_{i}^{\prime}
, and color
5
to
v_{K}^{\prime}
,
q_{i-1}^{\prime}
,
a^{\prime}
,
c
and
d^{\prime}
. So
F
is 5-colorable (see Figure
6
).

B_{i-2}
N_{B_{i-2}}(K)
q_{i-2}
B_{i-1}
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
q_{i-1}
q_{i-1}^{\prime}
a
a^{\prime}
B_{i}
N_{B_{i}}(v_{K}^{\prime\prime})
N_{B_{i}}(z)
b
q_{i}
q_{i}^{\prime}
B_{i+1}
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
q_{i+1}
c
B_{i+2}
N_{B_{i+2}}(J)
N_{B_{i+2}}(w)
d
d^{\prime}
q_{i+2}
K
v_{K}
v_{K}^{\prime}
J

Figure 6
:
Illustration of
F
and a 5-coloring of
F
. For convenience, all vertices of color 1 are shown in pink, all vertices of color 2 in green, all vertices of color 3 in blue, all vertices of color 4 in red and all vertices of color 5 in yellow. Note that
q_{i-1}^{\prime},v_{K}^{\prime}
and
a^{\prime}
may not exist.

Next, we show that every maximal clique of size
\omega-j
with
j\in\{0,1,2,3\}
of
G
contains at least
4-j
vertices from
F
. Let
M
be a maximal clique of
G
. We consider the following cases depending on all possible locations of
M
.

(i)
M\subseteq K\cup N_{H}(K)

We first assume that
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|\geq 2
. Then
q_{i-1}
,
q_{i-1}^{\prime}
and
v_{K}
are universal in
K\cup N_{H}(K)
by Lemmas
8.1
(2) and
8.3
. If
q_{i-2}
has non-neighbor
x\in K
, then
x-v_{K}-q_{i-2}-q_{i+2}-w-q_{i}-J
is an induced
P_{7}
. So,
q_{i-2}
is complete to
K
. Note that
b
is complete to
K
because
b\in N_{B_{i}}(v_{K}^{\prime\prime})
. Therefore,
M
contains either
\{q_{i-2},q_{i-1},q_{i-1}^{\prime},v_{K}\}
or
\{q_{i-1},q_{i-1}^{\prime},v_{K},b\}
.

We now assume that
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|=1
. Recall that
q_{i-1}
and
v_{K}
are universal in
K\cup N_{H}(K)
, and
q_{i-2}
and
b
is complete to
K
. If
v_{K}^{\prime}\in M
, then we are done. So we may assume that
v_{K}^{\prime}\notin M
. It follows that
|M\cap(K\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K))|=2
. As
|M\cap\{q_{i-2},q_{i-1},v_{K},b\}|=3
, we have
|M|=\omega
and so either
|M\cap N_{B_{i-2}}(K)|=\omega-2
or
|M\cap N_{B_{i}}(K)|=\omega-2
. If
|M\cap N_{B_{i-2}}(K)|=\omega-2
, then
N_{B_{i-2}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(w)
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
by Lemma
8.10
. If
|M\cap N_{B_{i}}(K)|=\omega-2
, then
N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i}}(z)\cup B_{i+1}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. Therefore,
v_{K}^{\prime}\in M
.

(ii)
M\subseteq J\cup N_{H}(J)

We first assume that
M\subseteq J\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. By Lemma
8.10
,
q_{i}
,
q_{i}^{\prime}
and
b
are universal in
J\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. So,
q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i+1},b\in M
.

We now assume that
M\subseteq J\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(J)
. Note that
d
is complete to
J
because
d\in N_{B_{i+2}}(v_{J}^{\prime\prime})
. So,
q_{i+1},d\in M
. Hence, we may assume that
|M|\geq\omega-1
and it follows that either
|M\cap(J\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J))|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
or
|M\cap N_{B_{i+2}}(J)|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
. Then either
N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i}}(z)\cup J\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
or
N_{B_{i+2}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(w)\cup B_{i-2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
by Lemma
8.10
, a contraction. Therefore,
|M|\leq\omega-2
and the claim holds.

(iii)
M\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}

Note that
q_{i-2}
and
q_{i-1}
are universal in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
. Suppose first that
a\in M
. If
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|\geq 2
, then
q_{i-1}^{\prime}\in M
because
N_{B_{i-2}}(a)\subseteq N_{B_{i-2}}(q_{i-1}^{\prime})
. So we may assume that
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|=1
. This implies that
|M|=\omega
and
a^{\prime}\notin M
. Then
|M\cap B_{i-2}|=\omega-2
and it follows that
N_{B_{i+2}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(w)\cup B_{i-2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction. So
a^{\prime}\in M
and we are done. Hence, we may assume that
a\notin M
. As
q_{i-2},q_{i-1}\in M
, we have
|M|\geq\omega-1
. Then either
|M\cap B_{i-2}|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
or
|M\cap N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
. If
|M\cap B_{i-2}|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
, then
N_{B_{i+2}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(w)\cup B_{i-2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. If
|M\cap N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
, then
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i}}(z)
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. Therefore,
|M|\leq\omega-2
and the claim holds.

(iv)
M\subseteq B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}

Note that
q_{i-1}
,
q_{i}
and
q_{i}^{\prime}
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
. So,
q_{i-1},q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
. Since
b
is universal in
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup B_{i}
, we have either
a\in M
or
b\in M
. In both case,
M
contains at least
4
vertices from
F
.

(v)
M\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}

By Lemma
8.10
,
q_{i}
,
q_{i}^{\prime}
and
b
are universal in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}
. So
q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i+1},b\in M
.

(vi)
M\subseteq B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}

Note that
q_{i+1}
and
q_{i+2}
are universal in
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
. So,
q_{i+1},q_{i+2}\in M
. Since
d
and
d^{\prime}
are universal in
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cup B_{i+2}
, we have either
c\in M
or
d,d^{\prime}\in M
. If
d,d^{\prime}\in M
, then we are done. So we may assume that
c\in M
. As
q_{i+1},q_{i+2},c\in M
, we have
|M|=\omega
. It follows that either
|M\cap B_{i+1}|\geq\frac{\omega}{2}
or
|M\cap N_{B_{i+2}}(w)|\geq\frac{\omega}{2}
. Then either
N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i}}(z)\cup B_{i+1}
or
N_{B_{i+2}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(w)\cup B_{i-2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
by Lemma
8.10
, a contradiction. Therefore,
|M|\leq\omega-1
and the claim holds.

(vii)
M\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}

By Lemma
8.10
,
d
and
d^{\prime}
are universal in
B_{i+2}\cup B_{i-2}
. So
q_{i+2},d,d^{\prime},q_{i-2}\in M
.

Therefore,
F
is a
(4,5)
-good subgraph.
∎

13.2
Distance 1

In this subsection, we handle the second outcome of Lemma
13.1
.

Lemma 13.3
.

Let
G=H\cup K\cup J
where
K
is a non-empty component of
A_{3}(i-2)
and
J
is a non-empty component of
A_{3}(i+2)
. Then
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

By Lemma
13.1
, both
K
and
J
are cliques. Moreover,
B_{i-2}\setminus N(K)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
and
B_{i+2}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. Let
z_{K}\in B_{i-2}\setminus N(K)
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i-2}\setminus N(K)
and let
z_{J}\in B_{i+2}\setminus N(J)
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i+2}\setminus N(J)
. Then
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i-1}}(z_{K})
are disjoint and
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(z_{J})
are disjoint. .

By Lemmas
8.15
and
8.14
,
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|
,
|N_{B_{i-1}}(z_{K})|
,
|N_{B_{i+1}}(J)|
,
|N_{B_{i+1}}(z_{J})|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. By Lemmas
8.11
and
8.14
, we have
|N_{B_{i-2}}(K)|\geq|N_{B_{i-2}}(J)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and
|N_{B_{i+2}}(J)|\geq|N_{B_{i+2}}(K)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. If there is an edge
uv
with
u\in B_{i-2}\setminus N(K)
and
v\in B_{i+2}\setminus N(J)
, then
K-N_{B_{i-1}}(K)-N_{B_{i-1}}(z_{K})-u-v-N_{B_{i+1}}(z_{J})-N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
B_{i-2}\setminus N(K)
is anticomplete to
B_{i+2}\setminus N(J)
.

Let
u_{K}\in K
be the vertex that has minimum neighborhood in
H
. Let
p\in N_{B_{i+2}}(u_{K})
. We show that
p
is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+1}
. Suppose not. By Lemma
8.1
(4), there is a vertex
x
in
N_{B_{i+2}}(K)
that is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+1}
. By assumption
x\notin N_{B_{i+2}}(u_{K})
. If
p
has non-neighbor
y\in B_{i+1}
, then
u_{K}-p-x-y
is a
P_{4}
-structure. So,
p
is complete to
B_{i+1}
. Hence, we may assume that
p
has a non-neighbor
y^{\prime}\in B_{i-2}
. Since
p
is complete to
N_{B_{i-2}}(K)
,
y^{\prime}\in B_{i-2}\setminus N(K)
. Then
u_{K}-p-x-y^{\prime}-N_{B_{i-1}}(y^{\prime})-N_{B_{i-1}}(u_{K})-u_{K}
is an induced
C_{6}
. So,
p
is complete to
B_{i-2}
.
Therefore,
p
is complete to
K\cup B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+1}
. We set this vertex to be
q_{i+2}
. By symmetry, there is a vertex in
N_{B_{i-2}}(J)
complete to
J\cup B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+2}
. We set this vertex to be
q_{i-2}
.

For
X\in\{K,J\}
, let
v_{X},v_{X}^{\prime}\in X
be the vertices with the largest and second-largest neighborhoods in
H
, respectively. Let
w_{K},w_{K}^{\prime}\in B_{i-2}\setminus N(K)
be the first and second maximal in
B_{i-2}\setminus N(K)
, respectively. Let
w_{J},w_{J}^{\prime}\in B_{i+2}\setminus N(J)
be the first and second maximal in
B_{i+2}\setminus N(J)
, respectively. Note that
v_{K}^{\prime},v_{J}^{\prime},w_{K}^{\prime}
and
w_{J}^{\prime}
may not exist. Let
a,b\in N_{B_{i-1}}(v_{K}^{\prime\prime})
where
v_{K}^{\prime\prime}\in K
that has a minimum neighborhood in
H
and let
c,d\in N_{B_{i+1}}(v_{J}^{\prime\prime})
where
v_{J}^{\prime\prime}\in J
that has a minimum neighborhood in
H
.

We set
F=(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3},F_{4},F_{5},O)
as follows:

•

F_{1}=\{q_{i-2},w_{K},w_{K}^{\prime}\}
,

•

F_{2}=\{q_{i-1},a,b\}
,

•

F_{3}=\{q_{i}\}
,

•

F_{4}=\{q_{i+1},c,d\}
,

•

F_{5}=\{q_{i+2},w_{J},w_{J}^{\prime}\}
,

•

O=\{v_{K},v_{K}^{\prime},v_{J},v_{J}^{\prime}\}
.

We can assign color
1
to
q_{i-1}
,
q_{i+1}
,
v_{K}
and
v_{J}
, color
2
to
q_{i}
,
v_{K}^{\prime}
,
v_{J}^{\prime}
,
w_{K}
and
w_{J}
, color
3
to
q_{i-2}
and
c
, color
4
to
q_{i+2}
and
a
, and color
5
to
w_{K}^{\prime}
,
w_{J}^{\prime}
,
b
and
d
. So
F
is 5-colorable (see Figure
7
).
