Figure 7
:
Illustration of
F
and a 5-coloring of
F
. For convenience, all vertices of color 1 are shown in pink, all vertices of color 2 in green, all vertices of color 3 in blue, all vertices of color 4 in red and all vertices of color 5 in yellow. Note that
v_{K}^{\prime}
,
v_{J}^{\prime}
,
w_{K}^{\prime}
and
w_{J}^{\prime}
may not exist.

Next, we show that every maximal clique of size
\omega-j
with
j\in\{0,1,2,3\}
of
G
contains at least
4-j
vertices from
F
. Let
M
be a maximal clique of
G
. We consider the following cases depending on all possible locations of
M
. Since
K
and
J
play symmetric roles, it suffices to consider
K\cup N_{H}(K)
,
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
,
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
and
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
.

(i)
M\subseteq K\cup N_{H}(K)
(or
J\cup N_{H}(J)
)

Note that
q_{i-2}
and
v_{K}
are universal in
K\cup N_{H}(K)
. We first assume that
M\subseteq K\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
. Since
a,b\in N_{B_{i-1}}(v_{K}^{\prime\prime})
,
a
and
b
are complete to
K
. By Lemma
8.1
,
a
and
b
are complete to
N_{B_{i-2}}(K)
. Hence,
q_{i-2},v_{K},a,b\in M
.

We now assume that
M\subseteq K\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(K)
. Note that
q_{i+2}
is universal in
K\cup N_{B_{i-2}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(K)
. So,
q_{i-2},q_{i+2},v_{K}\in M
. Suppose that
v_{K}^{\prime}\notin M
. This implies that
|M|=\omega
and we have either
|M\cap N_{B_{i-2}}(K)|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
or
|M\cap N_{B_{i+2}}(K)|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
. It follows that either
N_{B_{i-2}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(z_{K})
or
N_{B_{i+2}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(z_{J})
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction.

(ii)
M\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
(or
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
)

Note that
q_{i-2}
and
q_{i-1}
are universal in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
. So,
q_{i-2},q_{i-1}\in M
. Since
a
and
b
are universal in
N_{B_{i-2}}(K)\cup B_{i-1}
, we have either
a,b\in M
or
w_{K}\in M
. If
a,b\in M
, then we are done. So, we may assume that
w_{K}\in M
. Suppose that
w_{K}^{\prime}\notin M
. This implies that
|M|=\omega
. Then
(M\setminus\{w_{K}\})\cup\{a,b\}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction.

(iii)
M\subseteq B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
(or
B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}
)

Note that
q_{i-1}
and
q_{i}
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
. By Lemma
8.10
,
a
and
b
are complete to
B_{i}
and so
a
and
b
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
. Therefore,
q_{i-1},q_{i},a,b\in M
.

(vii)
M\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}

Note that
q_{i-2}
and
q_{i+2}
are universal in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
. So,
q_{i-2},q_{i+2}\in M
. Since
B_{i-2}\setminus N(K)
is anticomplete to
B_{i+2}\setminus N(J)
, either
M\subseteq N_{B_{i-2}}(K)\cup B_{i+2}
or
M\subseteq N_{B_{i+2}}(J)\cup B_{i-2}
. By symmetric, we may assume that
M\subseteq N_{B_{i-2}}(K)\cup B_{i+2}
. If
w_{J},w_{J}^{\prime}\in M
, then we are done. Let
j=|M\cap\{w_{J},w_{J}^{\prime}\}
. Then, we have
|M|=\omega-1+j
and it follows that either
|M\cap N_{B_{i-2}}(K)|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
or
|M\cap N_{B_{i+2}}(J)|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
. Then either
N_{B_{i-2}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(z_{K})
or
N_{B_{i+2}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(z_{J})
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
, a contradiction.

Therefore,
F
is a
(4,5)
-good subgraph.
∎

13.3
Distance 0

In this subsection, we handle the third outcome of Lemma
13.1
. We assume that
A_{3}=A_{3}(i)\neq\emptyset
has at most two components.
We first present several simple lemmas.

Lemma 13.4
.

There exists a clique of size larger than
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
in
B_{i-2}
complete to
B_{i+2}
.
By symmetry, there exists a clique of size larger than
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
in
B_{i+2}
complete to
B_{i-2}
.

Proof.

Let
v
be a vertex in
B_{i+2}
with minimal neighborhood in
H
.
By the minimality of
v
,
N(v)\setminus B_{i-2}
is a clique and so
N_{B_{i-2}}(v)
is a clique of size larger than
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
complete to
B_{i+2}
.
∎

Lemma 13.5
.

If
B_{i-1}
is complete to
B_{i-2}
and
B_{i+1}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
, then
B_{i-2}
and
B_{i+2}
are complete.

Proof.

Let
z\in B_{i-2}
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i-2}
and
z^{\prime}\in B_{i+2}\setminus N(z)
be a maximal vertex in
B_{i+2}\setminus N(z)
. Let
M\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
be a maximum clique of
G
.
We show that
M
contains one of
z
and
z^{\prime}
.
Suppose for a contradiction that
z,z^{\prime}\notin M
. If
M\cap B_{i+2}\subseteq N_{B_{i+2}}(z)
, then
M\cup\{z\}
is also a clique, a contradiction. So
(M\cap B_{i+2})\setminus N(z)\neq\emptyset
and let
a\in(M\cap B_{i+2})\setminus N(z)
. If
M\cap B_{i-2}\subseteq N_{B_{i-2}}(z^{\prime})
, then
M\cup\{z^{\prime}\}
is also a clique, a contradiction. So
(M\cap B_{i-2})\setminus N(z^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
and let
b\in(M\cap B_{i-2})\setminus N(z^{\prime})
. It follows that
ab\in E(G)
and
z^{\prime}b\notin E(G)
, which contradicts the choice of
z^{\prime}
. Then
\{z,z^{\prime},q_{i}\}
is a good stable set of
G
.
∎

Lemma 13.6
.

If
|N_{B_{i}}(A_{3}(i))|=1
, then every vertex in
(B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+1})\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
has a neighbor in
B_{i}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
.

Proof.

Let
K
be a non-empty component of
A_{3}(i)
and
J
be the possible second component of
A_{3}(i)
.
By Lemma
4.10
, we may assume that
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
.
Suppose that this lemma is not true.
We first assume that there exists
x\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
anticomplete to
B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
.
Then we may assume that
x
is minimal in
B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
.
By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
is complete to
B_{i+2}
.
If
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
, then
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
is complete to
B_{i+2}
.
If
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
, then by Lemma
8.1
(4), for any vertex
u\in N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and any vertex
v\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)
,
N(v)\cap H\subseteq N(u)\cap H
.
So
x
is also minimal in
B_{i+1}
. Hence,
B_{i+1}\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(x)
is a clique and so
d(x)\leq\omega
.
We now assume that there exists
x\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
anticomplete to
B_{i}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
. Let
y\in B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
. It suffices to show that
y
is minimal in
B_{i-1}
. By assumption,
y
is anticomplete to
B_{i}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
. Suppose that there exist
a\in B_{i-2}
and
b\in N_{B_{i-1}}(A_{3}(i))
such that
ab\notin E(G)
and
ay\in E(G)
. Let
c\in A_{3}(i)\cap N(b)
. Then
a-y-b-c
contradicts Lemma
4.11
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 13.7
.

Let
J_{1}
be a non-empty component of
A_{3}(i)
and
J_{2}
be the possible second component of
A_{3}(i)
.
Suppose that
B_{i}\setminus N(J_{s})
is anticomplete to
B_{i+1}\cap N(J_{s})
and
B_{i-1}\setminus N(J_{s})\neq\emptyset
for
s=1,2
.
If every vertex in
(B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i)))\cup(B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i)))
has a neighbor in
B_{i}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
, then
B_{j}
is complete to
B_{j+1}
for
j\neq i
.

Proof.

Note that
q_{i+1}\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))
. By assumption and Lemma

8.10
,
B_{i+1}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
.

Next we show that
B_{i-1}
is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
.
Suppose first that
B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))\neq\emptyset
.
By assumption and Lemma
8.7
,
B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
.
By Lemma
8.1
(4),
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
. Hence,
B_{i-1}
is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
.
Now suppose that
B_{i-1}\setminus N(A_{3}(i))=\emptyset
.
It follows that
J_{2}\neq\emptyset
,
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
have disjoint neighborhoods in
B_{i-1}
and
B_{i-1}=N_{B_{i-1}}(J_{1})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(J_{2})
.
By assumption and Lemma
8.7
,
B_{i-1}
is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
. By Lemma
13.5
,
B_{i-2}
and
B_{i+2}
are complete.
∎

We first deal with the following special case.

Lemma 13.8

(Coloring
H\cup A_{3}(i)
with four complete pairs)
.

Let
K
and
J
be two components of
A_{3}(i)

(
It is also possible that
J=\emptyset
)
and
G=H\cup K\cup J
. If
B_{j}
and
B_{j+1}
complete for
j\neq i
, then
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

We assume that
\omega(J)\leq\omega(K)
.
By assumption and Lemma
4.10
, we may assume that
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
, and
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. By Lemmas
8.14
and
13.4
, we have
|B_{j}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
for
j\in\{i-2,i-1,i+2\}
. By Lemma
8.6
, there exist vertices
w_{K}\in K
universal in
K
and
w_{j}\in J
universal in
J
such that
N_{H}(K)=N_{H}(w_{K})
and
N_{H}(J)=N_{H}(w_{J})
.
It follows from Lemma
8.14
that
|N_{B_{j}}(K)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and
|N_{B_{j}}(J)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
for
j\in\{i-1,i+1\}
.

Let
z\in B_{i}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
. Then,
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)\subseteq B_{i+1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
. Since every vertex in
N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)
has a neighbor in
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
, we obtain
z
is also minimal in
B_{i}
. By applying a small vertex argument,
|B_{i+1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. If
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
are disjoint, then
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
are comparable.

We now determine a maximum clique in
G
. By Lemma
8.1
(1),
q_{i}\in N_{B_{i}}(K)\cap N_{B_{i}}(J)
and
q_{i+1}\in B_{i+1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
. Let
p\in B_{i}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
be a maximal vertex in
B_{i}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
and let
r\in N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. Then,

•

If
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
is not a maximum clique, then
\{q_{i},q_{i+2}\}
is a good stable set.

•

If
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
is not a maximum clique, then
\{q_{i-2},q_{i+1},w_{K},w_{J}\}
is a good stable set.

•

If
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is not a maximum clique, then
\{q_{i-2},q_{i}\}
is a good stable set.

So
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
,
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
and
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
are maximum clique in
G
.

We now develop the coloring strategy. By Lemma
8.5
,
K
and
J
are
P_{4}
-free and so we have
\chi(K)=\omega(K)
and
\chi(J)=\omega(J)
. Let
\omega(K)=\alpha\omega
and
|B_{i-1}|=\beta\omega
. By Lemma
8.8
,
|B_{i}\setminus N(K)|\geq\alpha\omega
, and so we can assume that
|B_{i}\setminus N(K)|=(\alpha+\gamma)\omega
for some
\gamma\geq 0
. Observe that
\gamma\omega\in\mathbb{N}
because
\gamma\omega=|B_{i}\setminus N(K)|-\omega(K)
. Since
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
and
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
are maximum clique, this implies that
|B_{i-2}|=(1-\beta)\omega
and
|N_{B_{i}}(K)|=(1-\alpha-\beta-\gamma)\omega
. As
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
may not be a maximum clique, let
|B_{i+2}|=(\beta-\delta)\omega
with
\delta\geq 0
. Since
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is a maximum clique,
|B_{i+1}|=(1-\beta+\delta)\omega
. If
\omega(K)\geq\frac{3}{4}\omega
, then
|B_{i}\setminus N(K)|\geq\frac{3}{4}\omega
and this implies that
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So we may assume that
\omega(K)<\frac{3}{4}\omega
.

(i)
\frac{1}{4}\omega<\omega(K)<\frac{3}{4}\omega

We now partition
K
into three parts
(X,Y,Z)
of color sizes
x\omega
,
y\omega
, and
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
where
x\omega,y\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. Since
x\omega+y\omega=\alpha\omega-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil<\frac{1}{2}\omega
, this implies that we can choose
x\omega
and
y\omega
that satisfies
x\omega,y\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. We then color some vertices in
G
using
x\omega+y\omega+2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
colors as follows.

•

Assign the colors
a_{1},\ldots,a_{x\omega}
to the vertices of
X
, and
x\omega
vertices in
B_{i-2}
.

•

Assign the colors
b_{1},\ldots,b_{y\omega}
to the vertices of
Y
, and
y\omega
vertices in
B_{i+2}
.

•

Assign the colors
c_{1},\ldots,c_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil}
to the vertices of
Z
,
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices in
B_{i+1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
, and
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices in
B_{i-2}
.

•

Assign colors
d_{1},\ldots,d_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil}
to
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices in
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
, and
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
vertices in
B_{i-2}
.

•

The vertices of
J
are colored starting from
c_{1}
and proceeding sequentially through

c_{1},\ldots,c_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil},a_{1},\ldots,a_{x\omega},b_{1},\ldots,b_{y\omega}

using exactly
\omega(J)
colors.

•

Assign the colors
d_{1},\ldots,d_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil},a_{1},\ldots,a_{x\omega},b_{1},\ldots,b_{y\omega}
to
\alpha\omega
vertices in
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
in such a way that non-neighbors of
J
in
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
are colored first, in order.

|B_{i-2}|=(1-\beta)\omega
B_{i-2}
a_{1},\ldots,a_{x\omega}
c_{1},\ldots,c_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil}
d_{1},\ldots,d_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil}
|B_{i-1}|=\beta\omega
B_{i-1}
|B_{i}|=(1-\beta)\omega
B_{i}
b_{1},\ldots,b_{y\omega}
a_{1},\ldots,a_{x\omega}
d_{1},\ldots,d_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil}
|B_{i+1}|=(1-\beta+\delta)\omega
B_{i+1}
d_{1},\ldots,d_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil}
c_{1},\ldots,c_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil}
|B_{i+2}|=(\beta-\delta)\omega
B_{i+2}
b_{1},\ldots,b_{y\omega}
K
a_{1},\ldots,a_{x\omega}
b_{1},\ldots,b_{y\omega}
c_{1},\ldots,c_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil}
J
c_{1},\ldots,c_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil},
a_{1},\ldots,a_{x\omega},
b_{1},\ldots

Figure 8
:
The coloring strategy for Case (i). For each
j\in\{i,i+1\}
, the black line in
B_{j}
distinguishes
N_{B_{j}}(K)
from
B_{j}\setminus N(K)
. Two black line between
B_{j}
and
B_{j+1}
mean that the two sets are complete to each other. The remaining graph consists of the red-boxed parts.

The coloring strategy is illustrated in the above figure. By Lemma
4.13
,
N_{B_{i}}(K)\subseteq N_{B_{i}}(J)
or
N_{B_{i}}(J)\subseteq N_{B_{i}}(K)
. By Lemma
8.8
and
\omega(K)\geq\omega(J)
, the number of non-neighbors of
J
in
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
is at least
\omega(J)
and so the coloring used on
J\cup B_{i}
is proper. The remaining graph consists of the red-boxed parts in the figure, and it suffices to show that the remaining graph
G^{\prime}
is
\left(\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-x\omega-y\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\right)
-colorable. Since
|B_{i+1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))|,|N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(J)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, the each red-boxed parts in
B_{i+1}
have non-negative size. Since
|B_{i+2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, the red-boxed part in
B_{i+2}
also has non-negative size. Note that the red-boxed part in
B_{i-2}
could be empty. Then we only need to consider the worst case that
B_{i}
is complete to
B_{i+1}
in
G^{\prime}
and so
G^{\prime}
is a hyperhole
C_{5}[s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4},s_{5}]
, where

•

s_{1}=(1-\beta)\omega-x\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
,

•

s_{2}=\beta\omega
,

•

s_{3}=(1-\beta)\omega-x\omega-y\omega-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
,

•

s_{4}=(1-\beta+\delta)\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
,

•

s_{5}=(\beta-\delta)\omega-y\omega
.

We first show that
\omega(G^{\prime})\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-x\omega-y\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. It is equivalent to show that for every
j\in[5]
,

s_{j}+s_{j+1}\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-x\omega-y\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil

where the indices are taken modulo
5
(so that
s_{6}=s_{1}
). For
j=1,2,4,5
, the above inequality is equivalent to the following respective inequality

y\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\,,\,\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\,,\,x\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil\,,\,\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil+\delta\omega,

and in each case the inequality holds clearly. When
j=3
, the inequality yields
2\omega\leq 2\beta\omega+\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil+\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-\delta\omega
. Since
(\beta-\delta)\omega=|B_{i+2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and
\beta\omega=|B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, this inequality also holds for
j=3
. Hence, the inequality holds for all
j\in[5]
. Now we show that
\left\lceil\frac{|V(G^{\prime})|}{\alpha(G^{\prime})}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-x\omega-y\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. Observing that
\alpha(G^{\prime})=2
, it suffices to show

|V(G^{\prime})|\leq 2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-2x\omega-2y\omega-4\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil.

Since
|V(G^{\prime})|=3\omega-\beta\omega-2x\omega-2y\omega-5\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, we obtain
3\omega\leq\beta\omega+\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil+2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
and this inequality is satisfied because
\beta\omega\geq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil+1
. By Lemma
2.1
,
\chi(G^{\prime})=\max\{\omega(G^{\prime}),\left\lceil\frac{|V(G^{\prime})|}{\alpha(G^{\prime})}\right\rceil\}\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-x\omega-y\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.

(ii)
\omega(K)\leq\frac{1}{4}\omega

Recall that
z\in B_{i}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
and
|N_{B_{i+1}}(z)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. As
z
is minimal in
B_{i}
,
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
is complete to
B_{i}
. Since
N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
is a clique,
|N_{B_{i}}(K)|<\frac{\omega}{2}
.
Let
x\omega=\min\{|B_{i}\setminus N(K)|,\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil\}
. We then color some vertices of
G
using
\alpha\omega+x\omega+\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
colors as follows. Recall that
|B_{i}\setminus N(K)|=(\alpha+\gamma)\omega
.

•

Assign the colors
a_{1},\ldots,a_{\alpha\omega}
to the vertices of
K
,
\alpha\omega
vertices in
B_{i+1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
, and
\alpha\omega
vertices in
B_{i-2}
.

•

Assign the colors
a_{1},\ldots,a_{\omega(J)}
to the vertices of
J
.

•

Assign the colors
b_{1},\ldots,b_{x\omega}
to the vertices of
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
,
x\omega
vertices in
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
, and
x\omega
vertices in
B_{i-2}
.

•

Assign colors
c_{1},\ldots,c_{\alpha\omega}
to
\alpha\omega
vertices in
B_{i}
excluding the vertices that have already been colored with
b_{1},\ldots,b_{x\omega}
, and
\alpha\omega
vertices in
B_{i-2}
.

•

Assign colors
d_{1},\ldots,d_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-\alpha\omega}
to
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-\alpha\omega
vertices in
B_{i+1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
, and
\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-\alpha\omega
vertices in
B_{i-1}
.

|B_{i-2}|=(1-\beta)\omega
B_{i-2}
a_{1},\ldots,a_{\alpha\omega}
b_{1},\ldots,b_{x\omega}
c_{1},\ldots,c_{\alpha\omega}
|B_{i-1}|=\beta\omega
B_{i-1}
d_{1},\ldots,d_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-\alpha\omega}
|B_{i}|=(1-\beta)\omega
B_{i}
c_{1},\ldots,c_{\alpha\omega}
b_{1},\ldots,b_{x\omega}
|B_{i+1}|=(1-\beta+\delta)\omega
B_{i+1}
b_{1},\ldots,b_{x\omega}
a_{1},\ldots,a_{\alpha\omega}
d_{1},\ldots,d_{\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-\alpha\omega}
|B_{i+2}|=(\beta-\delta)\omega
B_{i+2}
K
a_{1},\ldots,a_{\alpha\omega}
J
a_{1},\ldots,a_{\omega(J)}

Figure 9
:
The coloring strategy for Case (ii). For each
j\in\{i,i+1\}
, the black line in
B_{j}
distinguishes
N_{B_{j}}(K)
from
B_{j}\setminus N(K)
. Two black line between
B_{j}
and
B_{j+1}
mean that the two sets are complete to each other. The remaining graph consists of the red-boxed parts.

The coloring strategy is illustrated in the above figure. The remaining graph
G^{\prime}
consists of the red-boxed parts in the figure, and it suffices to show that
G^{\prime}
is
\left(\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-\alpha\omega-z\omega-\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil\right)
-colorable. Since
|B_{i+1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
,
|N_{B_{i+1}}(K)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and
x\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, the each red-boxed parts in
B_{i+1}
have non-negative size. Since
|B_{i-1}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, the red-boxed parts in
B_{i-1}
has non-negative size. Note that the red-boxed part in
B_{i-2}
and
B_{i}
could be empty. Then we only need to consider the worst case that
B_{i}
is complete to
B_{i+1}
in
G^{\prime}
and so
G^{\prime}
is a hyperhole
C_{5}[s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4},s_{5}]
, where

•

s_{1}=(1-\beta)\omega-2\alpha\omega-x\omega
,

•

s_{2}=\beta\omega-\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil+\alpha\omega
,

•

s_{3}=(1-\beta)\omega-\alpha\omega-x\omega
,

•

s_{4}=(1-\beta+\delta)\omega-x\omega-\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
,

•

s_{5}=(\beta-\delta)\omega
.

We first show that
\omega(G^{\prime})\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-\alpha\omega-x\omega-\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
. It suffices to show that for every
j\in[5]
,

s_{j}+s_{j+1}\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-\alpha\omega-x\omega-\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil

where the indices are taken modulo
5
(so that
s_{6}=s_{1}
). From the
j=1,2,4,5
, the above inequality is equivalent to the following respective inequality

\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil\,,\,\alpha\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil\,,\,\alpha\omega\leq\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil\,,\,0\leq\alpha\omega+\delta\omega,

and in each case the inequality holds. When
j=3
, the inequality yields
2\omega\leq 2\beta\omega+x\omega+\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-\delta\omega
. Since
\beta\omega-\delta\omega=|B_{i+2}|>\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
and
\beta\omega+x\omega=\omega-|N_{B_{i}}(K)|>\frac{\omega}{2}
, this inequality also holds for
j=3
. Hence, the inequality holds for all
j\in[5]
. Now we show that
\left\lceil\frac{|V(G^{\prime})|}{\alpha(G^{\prime})}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-\alpha\omega-x\omega-\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
. Observing that
\alpha(G^{\prime})=2
, it suffices to show

|V(G^{\prime})|\leq 2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-2\alpha\omega-2x\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil.

Since
|V(G^{\prime})|=3\omega-\beta\omega-2\alpha\omega-3x\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
, the inequality is equivalent to
3\omega\leq\beta\omega+x\omega+2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
, which holds as
\beta\omega+x\omega>\frac{1}{2}\omega
.
By Lemma
2.1
,
\chi(G^{\prime})=\max\{\omega(G^{\prime}),\left\lceil\frac{|V(G^{\prime})|}{\alpha(G^{\prime})}\right\rceil\}\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-\alpha\omega-x\omega-\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.
∎

We then consider cases depending on the number of components of
A_{3}(i)
and the number of neighbors of each component in
B_{i}
.

Lemma 13.9

(One component with one neighbor in
B_{i}
)
.

If
A_{3}(i)
is connected and
|N(A_{3}(i))\cap B_{i}|=1
, then
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

For convenience, let
K=A_{3}(i)
.
By Lemma
8.6
, let
w\in K
be a universal vertex in
K
such that
N_{H}(w)=N_{H}(K)
. By Lemma
4.10
, we may assume that
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
. So
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)=\{q_{i}\}
.
By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. Let
X_{i+1}=B_{i+1}\setminus N(K)
. By Lemma
13.6
, each vertex in
X_{i+1}
has a neighbor in
B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
. By Lemma
8.10
,
X_{i+1}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. It follows that
B_{i+1}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
.

Suppose that
B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)\neq\emptyset
and let
X_{i-1}=B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
. By Lemma
13.6
, each vertex in
X_{i-1}
has a neighbor in
B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
. By Lemmas
13.7
and
13.8
, we have
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
. So we may assume that
X_{i-1}=\emptyset
.

Let
z\in B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
.
By Lemma
8.15
,
|N_{B_{i+1}}(z)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
By Lemma
13.4
,
|B_{j}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
for
j\in\{i-2,i+2\}
.
This implies that
\omega\geq 5
.
By Lemma
13.1
(3),
K
is a blowup of
P_{3}
and so
\alpha(K)\leq 2
.

Case 1:
K
contains two non-adjacent vertices.

Since
K
is a
P_{4}
-free, let
u
and
v
be two simplicial vertices in
K
. Let
u^{\prime}\in N_{G}[u]\cap K
be a vertex minimizing
|N_{H}(u^{\prime})|
and
v^{\prime}\in N_{G}[v]\cap K
be a vertex minimizing
|N_{H}(v^{\prime})|
. By Lemma
2.6
,
u^{\prime}v^{\prime}\notin E(G)
.
By Lemma
8.14
, we have
|N_{B_{j}}(u^{\prime})|,|N_{B_{j}}(v^{\prime})|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
for each
j\in\{i-1,i+1\}
. If
N_{B_{i+1}}(u^{\prime})
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(v^{\prime})
are disjoint, then
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So
N_{B_{i+1}}(u^{\prime})
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(v^{\prime})
are comparable. By Lemma
4.12
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(u^{\prime})
and
N_{B_{i-1}}(v^{\prime})
are disjoint. Since
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
are disjoint, we have
N_{B_{i-1}}(u^{\prime})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(v^{\prime})\subseteq N_{B_{i-1}}(z)
and it follows from the choice of
z
that
N_{B_{i-1}}(u^{\prime})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(v^{\prime})
is complete to
B_{i}
. By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(u^{\prime})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(v^{\prime})
is complete to
B_{i-2}
.

Note that
B_{i-1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
and
B_{i+1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
are not maximum clique, since
B_{i-1}\cup\{q_{i},w\}
and
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
are cliques. By the definition of
z
, there is a vertex in
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
complete to
B_{i}\cup B_{i+2}
. We set this vertex to be
q_{i+1}
. Let
a\in N_{B_{i-1}}(u^{\prime})
and let
b\in N_{B_{i-1}}(v^{\prime})
. Let
a^{\prime}\in K
be a vertex non-adjacent to
a
maximizing
|N_{H}(a^{\prime})|
and let
b^{\prime}\in K
be a vertex non-adjacent to
b
maximizing
|N_{H}(b^{\prime})|
.
If
a^{\prime}u^{\prime}\in E(G)
, then
a^{\prime}a\in E(G)
by the choice of
u
, a contradiction. So
a^{\prime}u^{\prime}\notin E(G)
and thus
a^{\prime}v^{\prime}\in E(G)
by
\alpha(K)\leq 2
. By symmetry,
b^{\prime}u^{\prime}\in E(G)
. Let
c\in N_{B_{i+1}}(u^{\prime})\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(v^{\prime})
. Then
c
is complete to
K
. Let
q_{j}^{\prime}\in B_{j}
be the second largest vertex in
B_{j}
for
j\in\{i-2,i+2\}
. By Lemma
13.4
, we have
q^{\prime}_{i+2}
is complete to
B_{i-2}
and
q^{\prime}_{i-2}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
.
Let
q^{\prime}_{i},q^{\prime\prime}_{i}
be the second and third largest vertices in
B_{i}
, respectively.

We set
F=(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3},F_{4},F_{5},O)
as follows:

•

F_{1}=\{q_{i-2},q_{i-2}^{\prime}\}
,

•

F_{2}=\{a,b\}
,

•

F_{3}=\{q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}
,

•

F_{4}=\{c,q_{i+1}\}
,

•

F_{5}=\{q_{i+2},q_{i+2}^{\prime}\}
,

•

O=\{w,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\}
.

We can assign color
1
to
q_{i-2}
and
q_{i}
, color
2
to
w
,
q_{i}^{\prime}
and
q_{i+2}
, color
3
to
a
,
a^{\prime}
and
q_{i+2}^{\prime}
, color
4
to
b
,
b^{\prime}
and
q_{i+1}
, and color
5
to
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
,
q_{i}^{\prime\prime}
and
c
. So
F
is 5-colorable. Next, we show that every maximal clique of size
\omega-j
with
j\in\{0,1,2,3\}
of
G
contains at least
4-j
vertices from
F
. Let
M
be a maximal clique of
G
. We consider the following cases depending on all possible locations of
M
. If
M
is contained in
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
or
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
,
M
contains four vertices of
F
.

(i)
M\subseteq K\cup N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{j}}(K)
for every
j\in\{i-1,i+1\}

Note that
w
and
q_{i}
are universal vertex in
K\cup N_{H}(K)
.

We first assume that
M
is contained in
K\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
. By definition of
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
,
M
contains at least one of
a
and
b
. If
a,b\in M
, then we are done. So we may assume that
M
contains exactly one of
a
and
b
. By symmetry, we may assume that
a\in M
and this implies that
M\cap N_{B_{i-1}}(v^{\prime})=\emptyset
. By definition of
b^{\prime}
, we have
b^{\prime}\in M
and so
M
contains
\{a,b^{\prime},q_{i},w\}
.

So we may assume that
M\subseteq K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
. Note that
q_{i},w,c
are universal in
K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
. Suppose that
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\notin M
. By definition of
a^{\prime}
and
b^{\prime}
,
M\cap K
is complete to
\{a,b\}
and it follows that
M\cap K
is complete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(u^{\prime})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(v^{\prime})
. As
(M\cap K)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(u^{\prime})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(v^{\prime})\cup\{q_{i}\}
is a clique and
|N_{B_{i-1}}(u^{\prime})\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(v^{\prime})|>2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, we obtain
|M\cap K|<\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-1
. So,
|M\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(K)|>2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. Hence,
M\cap\{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\}\neq\emptyset
and we are done.

(ii)
M\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
.

Since
a,b,q_{i-2}
are universal in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
,
a,b,q_{i-2}\in M
. Suppose that
|M|=\omega
and
q^{\prime}_{i-2}\notin M
. Then
M\cap B_{i-2}=\{q_{i-2}\}
and so
|M\cap B_{i-1}|=\omega-1
. Then
(M\cap B_{i-1})\cup\{q_{i},w\}
is a clique of size
\omega+1
, a contradiction.

(iii)
M\subseteq B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}

Note that
a,b,q_{i}
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
. If
M=B_{i-1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
, then
M
is not a maximum clique and
a,b,q_{i}\in M
. So we may assume that
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\})\neq\emptyset
and this implies that
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
. Hence,
M
contains
\{a,b,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}\}
.

(iv)
M\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}

Note that
q_{i}
and
q_{i+1}
are universal in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}
. If
M=B_{i+1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
, then
M
is not a maximum clique and
c,q_{i},q_{i+1}\in M
. So we may assume that
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\})\neq\emptyset
. Then
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
and it follows that
M\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(K)=\emptyset
. Thus, we can assume that
|M|=\omega
. If
q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\notin M
, then
|M\cap B_{i+1}|=\omega-2
and it follows that
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So,
q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in M
and the claim holds.

Therefore,
F
is a
(4,5)
-good subgraph. From now on, we may assume that
K
is a clique.

Case 2:

K
is a clique.

Suppose that
|K|=1
. For any
x\in N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
, we have
d_{G}(x)\leq(\omega-1)+2\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil-1
since
\omega\geq 5
. So we may assume that
|K|\geq 2
. By Lemma
8.8
, we have
|B_{i}|\geq 3
. Let
w^{\prime}\in K
be a vertex minimizing
|N_{H}(w^{\prime})|
. By Lemma
8.1
(4), there exists a vertex in
N_{B_{i-1}}(w^{\prime})
that is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
.
We set this vertex to be
q_{i-1}
. Moreover,
q_{i-1}
is complete to
K
.

Let
a\in N_{B_{i+1}}(w^{\prime})
. Then
a
is complete to
K
. Let
v\in K
be the second largest vertex in
K
(It is possible that
v=w^{\prime}
). Note that
q_{i+1}\in N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
. Let
q_{j}^{\prime}\in B_{j}
be the second largest vertex in
B_{j}
for
j\in\{i-2,i+2\}
. By Lemma
13.4
, we have
q^{\prime}_{i+2}
is complete to
B_{i-2}
. Let
q^{\prime}_{i},q^{\prime\prime}_{i}
be the second and third largest vertices in
B_{i}
, respectively.
Since
|B_{i-2}|\geq 3
, the third largest vertex
q_{i-2}^{\prime\prime}
in
B_{i-2}
exists. By Lemma
13.4
, we have
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
and
q_{i-2}^{\prime\prime}
are complete to
B_{i+2}
.

We set
F=(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3},F_{4},F_{5},O)
as follows:

•

F_{1}=\{q_{i-2},q_{i-2}^{\prime},q_{i-2}^{\prime\prime}\}
,

•

F_{2}=\{q_{i-1}\}
,

•

F_{3}=\{q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}
,

•

F_{4}=\{a,q_{i+1}\}
,

•

F_{5}=\{q_{i+2},q_{i+2}^{\prime}\}
,

•

O=\{w,v\}
.

We can assign color
1
to
q_{i-2}
and
q_{i}
, color
2
to
w
,
q_{i}^{\prime}
and
q_{i+2}
, color
3
to
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
,
v
and
q_{i+1}
, color
4
to
q_{i-1}
and
q_{i+2}^{\prime}
, and color
5
to
q_{i-2}^{\prime\prime}
,
q_{i}^{\prime\prime}
and
a
. So
F
is 5-colorable. Next, we show that every maximal clique of size
\omega-j
with
j\in\{0,1,2,3\}
of
G
contains at least
4-j
vertices from
F
. Let
M
be a maximal clique of
G
. We consider the following cases depending on all possible locations of
M
. If
M
is one of
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
and
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
,
M
contains four vertices of
F
.

(i)
M\subseteq K\cup N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{j}}(K)
for every
j\in\{i-1,i+1\}

We first assume that
M
is contained in
K\cup(N_{B_{i-1}}(K))\cup\{q_{i}\}
. Note that
q_{i-1}
,
q_{i}
and
w
are universal in
K\cup(N_{B_{i-1}}(K))\cup\{q_{i}\}
. So
q_{i-1},q_{i},w\in M
and we can assume that
|M|=\omega
. Suppose that
v\notin M
. Then there exists a vertex
x\in B_{i-1}\cap M
that is non-adjacent to
v
. If
x
has a non-neighbor
x^{\prime}\in B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
, then
v-w-x-q_{i-2}-q_{i+2}-q_{i+1}-x^{\prime}
is an induced
P_{7}
. By Lemma
8.1
(4),

B_{i-1}\cap M
is complete to
B_{i}
. Since
|B_{i-1}\cap M|=\omega-2
and
|B_{i}|\geq 3
,
(B_{i-1}\cap M)\cup B_{i}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So
v\in M
and
M
contains
\{q_{i-1},q_{i},w,v\}
.

We then assume that
M\subseteq K\cup(N_{B_{i+1}}(K))\cup\{q_{i}\}
. Note that
q_{i},w,a
are universal in
K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
. Suppose that
v\notin M
and so we can assume that
|M|=\omega
. It follows that
|B_{i+1}\cap M|=\omega-2
. Then
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. Therefore,
v\in M
and we are done.

(ii)
M\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}

Note that
q_{i-2}
and
q_{i-1}
are universal in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
. Define
q
to be
q_{i-2}^{\prime\prime}
if
|M|=\omega
and to be
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
if
|M|=\omega-1
. We show that
q\in M
, which completes the proof. Suppose for a contradiction that
q\notin M
. Then there exists a vertex
x\in B_{i-1}\cap M
such that
xq\notin E(G)
. If
x
has non-neighbor
x^{\prime}\in B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
, then
q-q_{i-2}-x-w-N_{B_{i+1}}(K)-q_{i+1}-x^{\prime}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
x
is complete to
B_{i}
. For any
y\in B_{i-1}\cap M
with
yq\in E(G)
, we have
N_{B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}}(x)\subseteq N_{B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}}(y)
and so
B_{i-1}\cap M
is complete to
B_{i}
. This implies that
(B_{i-1}\cup M)\cup B_{i}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. This completes the claim.

(iii)
M\subseteq B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}

Note that
q_{i-1}
and
q_{i}
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
.
Since
B_{i-1}\cup\{q_{i},w\}
is a clique,

M\cap(B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\})\neq\emptyset
and this implies that
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
. Suppose that
q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\notin M
. Then there exists a vertex
x\in M\cap B_{i-1}
such that
xq_{i}^{\prime\prime}\notin E(G)
. If
x
has a non-neighbor
x^{\prime}\in B_{i-2}
, then
x^{\prime}-q_{i-2}-x-w-N_{B_{i+1}}(K)-q_{i+1}-q_{i}^{\prime\prime}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So,
x
is complete to
B_{i-2}
. For any
y\in B_{i-1}\cap M
with
yq_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in E(G)
, we have
N_{B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}}(x)\subseteq N_{B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}}(y)
and so
B_{i-1}\cap M
is complete to
B_{i-2}
. This implies that
(B_{i-1}\cup M)\cup B_{i-2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. Therefore,
q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in M
and so
M
contains
\{q_{i-1},q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}
.

(iv)
M\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}

Note that
q_{i}
and
q_{i+1}
are universal in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}
. If
M=B_{i+1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
, then
M
is not a maximum clique and
a,q_{i},q_{i+1}\in M
. So we may assume that
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\})\neq\emptyset
. Then
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
and it follows that
M\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(K)=\emptyset
. We can assume that
|M|=\omega
. If
q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\notin M
, then
|M\cap B_{i+1}|=\omega-2
and it follows that
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So
q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in M
and the claim holds.

Therefore,
F
is a
(4,5)
-good subgraph.
∎

Next we deal with the case that
A_{3}(i)
is connected with at least two neighbors in
B_{i}
. The lemma below also works for the case that
A_{3}(i)
has two components with each component having at least two neighbors in
B_{i}
.

Lemma 13.10

(One or two components with two neighbors in
B_{i}
)
.

If each component of
A_{3}(i)
has at least two neighbors in
B_{i}
, then
G
has a
(4,5)
-good subgraph.

Proof.

Let
K
be a non-empty component of
A_{3}(i)
and
J
be a possible second component of
A_{3}(i)
.
By Lemma
13.1
,
K
can be partitioned into three disjoint cliques
L_{1},L_{2},L_{3}
such that
L_{2}
is complete to
L_{1}\cup L_{3}
and
L_{1}
is anticomplete to
L_{3}
.
We may assume that
L_{1}
is non-empty if
L_{3}
is non-empty.
By Lemma
13.1
,
L_{3}=\emptyset
if
J\neq\emptyset
.
Let
w\in K
be universal in
K
with maximum neighborhood in
H
(such a vertex exists by Lemma
8.6
).
By Lemma
8.14
,
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and so
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|\geq 2
. It follows that
\{w\}\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i}}(K)
is a clique and so
\omega\geq 5
. By Lemma
13.4
, we have
|B_{i-2}|,|B_{i+2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and so
|B_{i-2}|,|B_{i+2}|\geq 3
.

By Lemma
4.1
, we can order vertices in
B_{j}
as
b_{j}^{1},b_{j}^{2},\ldots,b_{j}^{|B_{i}|}=q_{j}
so that

N_{B_{j-1}\cup B_{j+1}}(b_{j}^{1})\subseteq N_{B_{j-1}\cup B_{j+1}}(b_{j}^{2})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq N_{B_{j-1}\cup B_{j+1}}(b_{j}^{|B_{j}|})=B_{j-1}\cup B_{j+1}
for every
j\in[5]
. Let
q_{j}^{\prime}=b_{j}^{|B_{j}|-1}
and
q_{j}^{\prime\prime}=b_{j}^{|B_{j}|-2}
for each
j
. By Lemma
8.5
,
K
and
J
are
P_{4}
-free. By Lemma
4.10
, we may assume that
B_{i}\setminus N(w)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(w)
and
q_{i}\in N_{B_{i}}(w)
. By Lemma
4.10
,
N_{B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+1}}(a)\subset N_{B_{i-1}\cup B_{i+1}}(b)
for any
a\in B_{i}\setminus N(K)
and any
b\in N_{B_{i}}(K)
. This implies that
q^{\prime}_{i}\in N_{B_{i}}(K)
. By symmetry,
q_{i},q^{\prime}_{i}\in N_{B_{i}}(J)
if
J\neq\emptyset
.
Suppose that
q^{\prime}_{i}
has a non-neighbor in
B_{i+1}\setminus N(K)
. Let
a
be a minimal vertex over all non-neighbors of
q^{\prime}_{i}
in
B_{i+1}\setminus N(K)
. By Lemma
8.1
(2),
a
is minimal over
B_{i+1}
and
a
is anticomplete to
J
.
It follows that

N_{B_{i}}(a)=\{q_{i}\}
and
N(a)\setminus B_{i}
is a clique.
So
d(a)\leq\omega
, a contradiction. Therefore,
q^{\prime}_{i}
is complete to
B_{i+1}\setminus N(K)
and so
q^{\prime}_{i}
complete to
B_{i+1}
by Lemma
8.1
(2).

Let
z\in J
be universal in
J
with maximum neighborhood in
H
(such a vertex exists by Lemma
8.6
) if
J\neq\emptyset
. By Lemma
8.14
,
|N_{B_{i+1}}(K)|,|N_{B_{i+1}}(J)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. By Lemma
4.13
,
N_{B_{i}}(K)\subseteq N_{B_{i}}(J)
or
N_{B_{i}}(J)\subseteq N_{B_{i}}(K)
.
Let
w_{0}\in L_{1}\cup L_{2}
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood on
H
and
z_{0}\in J
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
H
. Let
a\in N_{B_{i-1}}(w_{0})
with maximum neighborhood in
H
and let
a^{\prime}\in N_{B_{i-1}}(z_{0})
with maximum neighborhood in
H
.
By Lemma
8.1
(4),
a
and
a^{\prime}
are complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
. If
J\neq\emptyset
, then
K
and
J
are cliques by Lemma
13.1
and so
w_{0}
and
z_{0}
are minimum and
a
is complete to
K
and
a^{\prime}
is complete to
J
.

Next we deduce some structural information about
J
and its neighbors on
H
when
J\neq\emptyset
. We partition
B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
into two subsets
X
and
Y
where every vertex in
X
has a neighbor in
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
and
Y
is anticomplete to
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
. By Lemma
8.7
,
X
is complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
. Let
x\in B_{i}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
.

Case 1:
J\neq\emptyset
and
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)

Then
N_{B_{i+1}}(x)
is disjoint from both
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
. By Lemma
8.15
,
|N_{B_{i+1}}(x)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Assume that
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
are disjoint. By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(x)
is complete to
B_{i+2}
and so
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(x)\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. Hence, we may assume that
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
are comparable.
By symmetry, we may assume that
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\subseteq N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
.
By Lemma
8.1
(2),
N_{B_{i}}(K)=N_{B_{i}}(J)
.

Case 2:
J\neq\emptyset
and
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
.

By symmetry, we may assume that
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
are disjoint. So, by Lemma
4.12
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
are comparable. By Lemma
8.1
(4), there exists a vertex in
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
. By assumption of
J
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\subsetneq N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
. By Lemma
8.1
(2),
N_{B_{i}}(K)\subseteq N_{B_{i}}(J)
.

Since
N_{B_{i-1}}(x)
and
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
are disjoint,
N_{B_{i+1}}(x)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
are comparable and so
q_{i+1}\in N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. Let
b\in B_{i}\setminus N(K)
be a maximal vertex in
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
. If
N_{B_{i}}(K)=N_{B_{i}}(J)
, then
b\in B_{i}\setminus N(J)
. If
N_{B_{i}}(K)\neq N_{B_{i}}(J)
, then
b\in N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus N_{B_{i}}(K)
because
N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus N_{B_{i}}(K)
is complete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
.

We show that
X=\emptyset
. If not, let
x^{\prime}
be a vertex in
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
with maximum neighborhood in
H
. Since
N_{B_{i+1}}(x^{\prime})
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
are disjoint,
N_{B_{i-1}}(x^{\prime})
and
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
are comparable. Since
X\subseteq N_{B_{i-1}}(x^{\prime})
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\subseteq N_{B_{i-1}}(x^{\prime})
and so
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\subseteq N_{B_{i-1}}(x^{\prime})
. This contradicts the assumption of this case.

Next, we will use the structure information in Cases 1 and 2 to define good subgraphs.
Since
|B_{i-2}|,|B_{i+2}|\geq 3
,
q^{\prime}_{j}
and
q^{\prime\prime}_{j}
exist for
j\in\{i-2,i+2\}
. We set
F=(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3},F_{4},F_{5},O)
as follows:

•

F_{5}=\{q_{i+2},q_{i+2}^{\prime}\}
,

•

F_{4}=\{q_{i+1},c\}
where
c
is a vertex in
N_{B_{i+1}}(z_{0})
if
Case 1
and
c\in N_{B_{i+1}}(w_{0})
is a vertex with maximum neighborhood in
H
if
Case 2
or
J=\emptyset
,

•

F_{3}=\{q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},b\}
where
b\in B_{i}\setminus N(K)
is a maximal vertex in
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
,

•

F_{2}
is defined as follows. Let
d\in Y
be a vertex with maximum neighborhood in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
if
Y\neq\emptyset
.

–

If
J=\emptyset
or
Case 2
, then

F_{2}=\begin{cases}\{a\},&Y=\emptyset\\
\{a,d\},&Y\neq\emptyset\end{cases}

–

If
Case 1
, then
F_{2}=\{a,a^{\prime}\}
.

•

F_{1}
is defined as follows. Let
e\in B_{i-2}\setminus N(d)
with maximal neighborhood in
B_{i-2}\setminus N(d)
if
Y\neq\emptyset
.

–

If
J=\emptyset
or
Case 2
, then

F_{1}=\begin{cases}\{q_{i-2},q_{i-2}^{\prime},q_{i-2}^{\prime\prime}\},&Y=\emptyset\\
\{q_{i-2},q_{i-2}^{\prime},e\},&Y\neq\emptyset\end{cases}

–

If
Case 1
, then
F_{1}=\{q_{i-2},q_{i-2}^{\prime}\}
.

•

–

O=\{w,v_{k}\}
if
J=\emptyset
, where
v_{k}
is a vertex in
L_{3}
with maximal neighborhood in
H
. Note that
v_{k}
may not exist.

–

O=\{w,z\}
if
Case 1
or
Case 2
with
N_{B_{i}}(K)\neq N_{B_{i}}(J)

–

O=\{w,z,z^{\prime}\}
if
Case 2
with
N_{B_{i}}(K)=N_{B_{i}}(J)
where
z^{\prime}\in J
such that
z^{\prime}
is second maximal in
H
.

We now show that
F
is 5-colorable. We first consider
Case 1
. In this case, we can assign color
1
to
q_{i-2},q_{i}
, color
2
to
a^{\prime},q_{i+1},w
, color
3
to
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
,
b
,
c
, color
4
to
a
,
q_{i+2}
,
z
, and color
5
to
q_{i}^{\prime}
and
q_{i+2}^{\prime}
.
In
Case 2
, we assign color
1
to
q_{i-2},q_{i}
, color
2
to
a^{\prime}
,
q_{i+1}
,
w
,
q_{i-2}^{\prime\prime}
,
d,e
and color
3
to
z^{\prime}
,
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
,
b,c
, color
4
to
a
,
q_{i+2},z
, and color
5
to
q_{i}^{\prime}
and
q_{i+2}^{\prime}
.
If
J=\emptyset
, we assign color
1
to
q_{i-2},q_{i}
, color
2
to
q_{i-2}^{\prime},q_{i+1},w
, color
3
to
q_{i-2}^{\prime\prime},b,c,d,e
, color
4
to
a,q_{i+2}
, and color
5
to
q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i+2}^{\prime}
. If
v_{k}
does not exist, the coloring is proper. So assume that
v_{k}
exists.
By the definition of
L_{1}
and
L_{3}
,
w_{0}\in L_{1}
and so either
a
or
c
is not adjacent to
v_{k}
.
We assign to
v_{k}
the color of a non-neighbor of
v_{k}
in
\{a,c\}
.
Therefore,
F
is 5-colorable.

Next, we show that every maximal clique of size
\omega-j
with
j\in\{0,1,2,3\}
of
G
contains at least
4-j
vertices from
F
. Let
M
be a maximal clique of
G
. We consider the following cases depending on all possible locations of
M
.

(i)
M\subseteq K\cup N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{j}}(K)
for every
j\in\{i-1,i+1\}

Since
\{w\}\cup N_{B_{i}}(w)
dominates
K\cup N_{H}(K)
, every maximal clique in
K\cup N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{j}}(K)
contains
\{w,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}\}
. We first consider
Case 2
or
J=\emptyset
.
If
J\neq\emptyset
, every maximum clique in
K\cup N_{B_{i}}(K)\cup N_{B_{j}}(K)
contains
\{a,c\}\cap B_{j}
by choice of
a
and
c
.
So we may assume that
J=\emptyset
.

We now consider
Case 1
. Suppose that
c
has a neighbor
w_{1}\in K
and a non-neighbor
w_{2}\in K
. Then
w_{2}-w_{1}-c-q_{i+1}-(B_{i}\setminus N(K))-a^{\prime}-q_{i-2}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
c
is complete to
K
. Since
a
is complete to
L_{1}\cup L_{2}
by choice of
a
, every maximum clique in
K\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i}}(K)
contains exactly one of
\{a,v_{k}\}
. This completes the proof of this case.

(ii)
M\subseteq J\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)\cup N_{B_{j}}(J)
for every
j\in\{i-1,i+1\}

In this case,
J\neq\emptyset
.
We first consider the case that
N_{B_{i}}(K)\neq N_{B_{i}}(J)
. Then, we have
N_{B_{i}}(K)\subsetneq N_{B_{i}}(J)
and since
N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus N_{B_{i}}(K)
is complete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
, we have
b\in N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus N_{B_{i}}(K)
. So, since
\{z\}\cup N_{B_{i}}(z)
dominates
J\cup N_{H}(J)
, every maximal clique in
J\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)\cup N_{B_{j}}(J)
contains
\{z,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},b\}
.

We now consider the case that
N_{B_{i}}(K)=N_{B_{i}}(J)
. Note that
q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},z\in M
. In
Case 1
, every maximum clique in
J\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)\cup N_{B_{j}}(J)
contains
\{a^{\prime},c\}\cap B_{j}
by choice of
a^{\prime}
and
c
. In
Case 2
, suppose that
z^{\prime}\notin M
.
Then we have
|M|=\omega
and
|M\setminus J|=\omega-1
.
Hence, either
(M\setminus J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
or
(M\setminus J)\cup\{a,w\}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
and so
z^{\prime}\in M
. This completes the proof of this case.

(iii)
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}

Since
q_{i-2}
and
a
are universal in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
,
q_{i-2},a\in M
.
So we may assume that
|M|\geq\omega-1
. We first consider
Case 1
. Since
a^{\prime}
is universal in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
,
a^{\prime}\in M
. Next, we show that
q_{i-2}^{\prime}\in M
, which will complete the proof for this case. Suppose for a contradiction that
q_{i-2}^{\prime}\notin M
.
Suppose first that
B_{i-1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))\neq\emptyset
.
Let
v\in B_{i-1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
.
By Lemma
8.1
(4),
v
is a vertex in
B_{i-1}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
.
It follows that
N(v)\setminus B_{i-2}
is a clique.
Thus,
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
is complete to
B_{i-1}
and so
q_{i-2}^{\prime}\in M
, a contradiction.
So
B_{i-1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))=\emptyset
.
By Lemma
8.1
(2),
q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}
are complete to
B_{i-1}
.
Then
|M\cap B_{i-2}|=1
and
|M\cap B_{i-1}|=\omega-1
.
So
(M\setminus\{q_{i-2}\})\cup\{q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}\}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
.

Next we consider
Case 2
or
J=\emptyset
.
We first consider the case
Y=\emptyset
.
Define
q
to be
q_{i-2}^{\prime\prime}
if
|M|=\omega
and to be
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
if
|M|=\omega-1
. We show that
q\in M
, which completes the proof. Suppose for a contradiction that
q\notin M
. Then there exists a vertex
u\in M\cap B_{i-1}
such that
uq\notin E(G)
.
By Lemma
8.7
,
u\in N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
. If
u
has non-neighbor
v
in
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
, then
q-q_{i-2}-u-w-N_{B_{i+1}}(K)-q_{i+1}-v
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
u
is complete to
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
. For any
x\in M\cap N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
with
xq\in E(G)
, we have
N_{B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}}(u)\subseteq N_{B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}}(x)
and so
M\cap N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
is complete to
B_{i}
. By Lemma
8.7
and
Y=\emptyset
,
M\cap(B_{i-1}\setminus N(K))
is complete to
B_{i}
.
This implies that
(M\cap B_{i-1})\cup\{q_{i},q^{\prime}_{i},b\}
is a clique of size
\omega+1
.

We now consider the case
Y\neq\emptyset
. Let
y\in Y
be minimal in
Y
. Since
Y
is anticomplete to
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
,
y
is also minimal in
B_{i-1}
. So
N(y)\setminus B_{i-2}
is a clique and thus
|N_{B_{i-2}}(y)|>\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
. In particular,
|N_{B_{i-2}}(y)|\geq 2
and so
q^{\prime}_{i-2}\in N_{B_{i-2}}(y)
. Therefore,
q_{i-2},q_{i-2}^{\prime},a\in M
. If
M\cap Y\neq\emptyset
, then
d\in M
, since
d
is maximal in
Y
. Assume that
M\cap Y=\emptyset
. If
M\cap B_{i-2}\subseteq N(d)
, then
M\cup\{d\}
is a clique, a contradiction. So
(M\cap B_{i-2})\setminus N(d)\neq\emptyset
. By the choice of
e
, we have
e\in M
. This completes the proof of this case.

(iv)
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}

We first consider
Case 1
. Since
a,a^{\prime},q_{i}
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
,
a,a^{\prime},q_{i}\in M
.
Suppose first that
B_{i-1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))\neq\emptyset
.
Let
v\in B_{i-1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
be a vertex with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
.
By Lemma
8.1
(4),
v
is a vertex in
B_{i-1}
with minimal neighborhood in
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
.
It follows that
N(v)\setminus B_{i}
is a clique.
Thus,
q_{i}^{\prime}
is complete to
B_{i-1}
and so
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
.
So
B_{i-1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))=\emptyset
.
By Lemma
8.1
(2),
q_{i}^{\prime}
is complete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(J)=B_{i-1}
and so
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
.

We then consider
Case 2
or
J=\emptyset
.
We first consider the case
Y=\emptyset
. Assume that
X\neq\emptyset
. By Lemma
8.1
(4) and Lemma
8.7
,
M=B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
contains
\{a,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},b\}
. Next we assume that
X=\emptyset
. Note that
a,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
and so
a,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
.
If
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus N(K))=\emptyset
, then
M\cup\{w\}
is a clique, a contradiction. So
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus N(K))\neq\emptyset
and this implies that
b\in M
. We now consider the case
Y\neq\emptyset
. Let
y\in Y
be minimal in
Y
. By
Y
is anticomplete to
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
and
P_{4}
-structure,
y
is also minimal in
B_{i-1}
. So
N(y)\setminus B_{i}
is a clique and so
|N(y)\cap N_{B_{i}}(K)|>\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.
So
q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
. By the choice of
a
, we have
a,q_{i},q^{\prime}_{i}\in M
. We assume that
X\neq\emptyset
. Recall that
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
is complete to
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
and so
X\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup B_{i}
is a clique. If
M\cap Y=\emptyset
, then
M
contains
\{a,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},b\}
. If
M\cap Y\neq\emptyset
, then
d\in M
by the choice of
d
. In this case, every maximal clique in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
contains one of
\{a,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},b\}
and
\{a,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},d\}
.
So
X=\emptyset
. Since
\{w\}\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(w)\cup N_{B_{i}}(w)
is a clique,
M\cap Y\neq\emptyset
or
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus N(K))\neq\emptyset
. If
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus N(K))\neq\emptyset
, then
b\in M
by the choice of
b
. If
M\cap Y\neq\emptyset
,
d\in M
by the choice of
d
.

(v)
M\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}

Note that
q_{i}
and
q_{i+1}
are universal in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}
and so
q_{i},q_{i+1}\in M
. Since
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
, it follows that
M
contains no vertex from
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
or
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
.
Since
q^{\prime}_{i}
is complete to
B_{i+1}
,
q^{\prime}_{i}\in M
. If
M
contains no vertices from
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
, then
M
contains a vertex in
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
(for otherwise
\{c\}\cup M
is a clique, which contradicts the maximality of
M
). By the choice of
b
,
b\in M
. If
M
contains no vertices from
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
, then
c\in M
since
c
is universal in
(B_{i}\cup B_{i+1})\setminus(B_{i}\setminus N(K))
. So
|M\cap F|=4
.

(vi)
M\subseteq B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}

Since
q_{i+1},q_{i+2},c
are universal in
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
,
q_{i+1},q_{i+2},c\in M
.
Suppose that
|M|=\omega
and
q^{\prime}_{i+2}\notin M
.
It follows that
M\cap B_{i+2}=\{q_{i+2}\}
and
M=B_{i+1}\cup\{q_{i+2}\}
. So
|B_{i+1}|=\omega-1
.
Since
B_{i+1}
is complete to
\{q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}\}
,
|B_{i+1}\cup\{q_{i},q^{\prime}_{i}\}|\geq\omega+1
, a contradiction.

(vii)
M\subseteq B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}

By Lemma
13.4
,
M
contains
\{q_{i-2},q_{i-2}^{\prime},q_{i+2},q_{i+2}^{\prime}\}
.

Therefore,
F
is a
(4,5)
-good subgraph.
∎

Finally, we deal with the case that
A_{3}(i)
has two components and one of the components has only one neighbor in
B_{i}
.

Theorem 13.11

(Two components with one having one neighbor in
B_{i}
)
.

Let
K
and
J
be two non-empty clique components of
A_{3}(i)
. If
|N_{B_{i}}(K)|=1
, then
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Proof.

Note that
N_{B_{i}}(K)=\{q_{i}\}
.
By Lemma
4.10
, we may assume that
B_{i}\setminus N(K)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
. By Lemma
13.4
, we have
|B_{i-2}|,|B_{i+2}|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
.
Let
w_{K}
and
w_{J}
be a maximum vertex in
K
and
J
, respectively.
Let
w_{K}^{\prime}
and
w_{J}^{\prime}
be a minimum vertex in
K
and
J
, respectively. It follows that
|N_{B_{j}}(w_{K}^{\prime})|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
and
|N_{B_{j}}(w_{J}^{\prime})|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
for
j\in\{i-1,i+1\}
.

Let
z\in B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
. Since
N_{B_{i}}(J)
is complete to
N_{B_{i\pm 1}}(J)
but
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is not, it follows that
z\in B_{i}\setminus N(J)
. By assumption,
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
are disjoint. As
z
is minimal in
B_{i}
, we have
N_{B_{i-1}}(z)\cup B_{i}
is a clique and so
|N_{B_{i+1}}(z)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
by Lemma
8.15
.
By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
are complete to
B_{i+2}
.
This implies that
\omega\geq 6
.
If
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
are disjoint, then
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(z)\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
by
8.10
. Therefore,
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
is comparable with
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
or
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
.

Case 1:

N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(z)\subseteq N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
.

Then
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(z)
are disjoint.
Since
z\in B_{i}\setminus N(J)
,
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
. If
|N_{B_{i}}(J)|\geq 2
, then there is a vertex
x\in N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus N_{B_{i}}(K)
that is complete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
, a contradiction. So
N_{B_{i}}(J)=N_{B_{i}}(K)=\{q_{i}\}
.

Suppose that
B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)\neq\emptyset
. By Lemma
4.12
,
B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
is anticomplete to
B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)
. Let
x\in B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)
be a minimal vertex in
B_{i+1}\setminus N(J)
. If
B_{i+1}\cup N_{B_{i+2}}(x)
contains two non-adjacent vertices
u
and
v
, then
u\in N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\setminus(N(K)\cup N(z))
and
v\in B_{i+2}
, then
w_{J}-u-x-v
contradicts Lemma
4.11
.
Since
N_{B_{i}}(x)=\{q_{i}\}
,
d(x)\leq\omega
, a contradiction. This show that
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)=B_{i+1}
.

By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
are complete to
B_{i-2}
.
Let
X_{i-1}=B_{i-1}\setminus(N(K)\cup N(J))
. By Lemma
13.6
, every vertex in
X_{i-1}
has a neighbor in
B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
.
By Lemma
8.7
,
X_{i-1}
is complete to
B_{i-2}
and it follows that
B_{i-1}
is complete to
B_{i-2}
.

Recall that
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
are complete to
B_{i+2}
. If
y\in B_{i+1}=N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
has a non-neighbor
y^{\prime}\in B_{i+2}
, then
y^{\prime}-q_{i+2}-y-J-N_{B_{i-1}}(J)-N_{B_{i-1}}(K)-K
is an induced sequence. So
B_{i+1}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. By Lemma
13.5
,
B_{i+2}
is complete to
B_{i-2}
. Since
|B_{i-2}|,|B_{i+2}|<\frac{\omega}{2}
,
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
is not a maximum clique.
By Lemma
8.7
,
X_{i-1}
is complete to
B_{i}
.

We now show that
K
is complete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
. If
x\in N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
has a non-neighbor
x^{\prime}\in K
, then
x^{\prime}-w_{K}-x-q_{i-2}-q_{i+2}-N_{B_{i+1}}(z)-w_{J}
is an induced
P_{7}
. So
K
is complete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
and
K\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
is a clique.

Suppose that
J\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)
contains a maximum clique. It follows that
|N_{B_{i-1}}(J)|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
or
|J|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
. If
|N_{B_{i-1}}(J)|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
, then
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So
|J|\geq\frac{\omega-1}{2}
. As
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(z)\subseteq N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
,
J
is complete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
. Since
|N_{B_{i+1}}(K)|,|N_{B_{i+1}}(z)|>\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, we obtain
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(z)\cup J
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. This proves that
J\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)
does not contain a maximum clique.
If
X_{i-1}\neq\emptyset
, then
\{x_{i-1},w_{K},q_{i+1}\}
is a good stable set where
x_{i-1}\in X_{i-1}
(rely on
J
is complete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
).
So
X_{i-1}=\emptyset
.

Note that
B_{i-1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
and
B_{i+1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
are not maximum clique.
Let
q^{\prime}_{i-1}\in B_{i-1}
be a common neighbor of
w_{K}^{\prime}
and
z
. It follows that
q^{\prime}_{i-1}
is complete to
B_{i}
and
K
. Let
a\in N_{B_{i-1}}(w_{J}^{\prime})
. Then
a
is complete to
J
. Let
b\in N_{B_{i+1}}(w_{K}^{\prime})
. Then
b
is complete to
K
. Let
v_{K}\in K
be the second largest vertex in
K
if
|K|\geq 2
. Let
q^{\prime}_{j}\in B_{j}
be the second largest vertex in
B_{j}
for
j\in\{i-2,i+2\}
. Let
q^{\prime}_{i},q^{\prime\prime}_{i}
be the second and third largest vertices in
B_{i}
, respectively.

We set
F=(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3},F_{4},F_{5},O)
as follows:

•

F_{1}=\{q_{i-2},q_{i-2}^{\prime}\}
,

•

F_{2}=\{q^{\prime}_{i-1},a\}
,

•

F_{3}=\{q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}
,

•

F_{4}=\{b,q_{i+1}\}
,

•

F_{5}=\{q_{i+2},q_{i+2}^{\prime}\}
,

•

O=\{w_{K},v_{K},w_{J}\}
.

We can assign color
1
to
q_{i-2}
and
q_{i}
, color
2
to
w_{K}
,
w_{J}
,
q_{i}^{\prime}
and
q_{i+2}
, color
3
to
a
,
q_{i}^{\prime\prime}
and
b
, color
4
to
v_{K}
,
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
and
q_{i+1}
, and color
5
to
q_{i-1}^{\prime}
and
q_{i+2}^{\prime}
. So
F
is 5-colorable.

Next, we show that every maximal clique of size
\omega-j
with
j\in\{0,1,2,3\}
of
G
contains at least
4-j
vertices from
F
. Let
M
be a maximal clique of
G
. We consider the following cases depending on all possible locations of
M
. If
M
is one of
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
,
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
and
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
,
M
contains four vertices of
F
.

(i)
M\subseteq K\cup N_{H}(K)

We first assume that
M
is contained in
K\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
. As
K\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
is a clique,
M
contains
\{w_{K},q_{i-1}^{\prime},q_{i}\}
.
If
v_{K}\notin M
, then
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|\geq\omega-2
and so
B_{i-1}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
as
|N_{B_{i-1}}(J)|\geq 3
.
So we may assume that
M\subseteq K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
. Note that
q_{i},w_{K},b
are universal in
K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
. So we can assume that
|M|=\omega
. If
v_{K}\notin M
, then
|N_{B_{i+1}}(K)|\geq\omega-2
and so
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
as
|N_{B_{i+1}}(z)|\geq 3
and
|B_{i+2}|\geq 3
. Therefore,
M
contains
\{w_{K},v_{K},q_{i},b\}
.

(ii)
M\subseteq J\cup N_{H}(J)

Suppose first that
M\cap B_{i+1}=\emptyset
.
Since
w_{J},q_{i},a
are universal in
J\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup\{q_{i}\}
,
w_{J},q_{i},a\in M
. Since
J\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup\{q_{i}\}
does not contain a maximum clique of
G
, we are done in this case. So
M\subseteq J\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cup\{q_{i}\}
. As
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
is complete to
J
,
M
contains
\{w_{J},b,q_{i},q_{i+1}\}
.

(iii)
M\subseteq B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
(
M\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}
is symmetric)

Since
B_{i-1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
is not a maximum clique,
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\})\neq\emptyset
and so
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
. Then,
M\cap N_{B_{i-1}}(J)=\emptyset
. Since
q_{i},q^{\prime}_{i-1}
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
,
q^{\prime}_{i-1},q_{i}\in M
. So we can assume that
|M|=\omega
.
If
q^{\prime\prime}_{i}
is not in
M
, then
|M\cap(B_{i-1}\setminus N(J))|\geq\omega-2
. Since
|B_{i-2}|\geq 3
,
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i-2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. So
M
contains
\{q_{i-1},q_{i},q^{\prime}_{i},q^{\prime\prime}_{i}\}
.

Therefore,
F
is a
(4,5)
-good subgraph. Hence, we may assume that
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
is comparable with exactly one of
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
, and is disjoint from the other.

Case 2:

N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
are comparable.

Then
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
are disjoint. If
|N_{B_{i}}(J)|\geq 2
, then there is a vertex
x\in N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus N_{B_{i}}(K)
that is complete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
, a contradiction. So we obtain
|N_{B_{i}}(J)|=1
and this implies that
N_{B_{i}}(J)=N_{B_{i}}(K)=\{q_{i}\}
. If
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
, then
z-q_{i-1}-N_{B_{i-1}}(J)-J-(N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(J))-N_{B_{i+1}}(z)-z
is an induced cyclic sequence. So
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. By Lemmas
13.6
,
13.7
and
13.8
,
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega\right\rceil
.

Case 3:

N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(z)
are comparable.

Then
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
are disjoint and it follows that
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(z)\subseteq N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
. So
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|>2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. Since
z\in B_{i}\setminus N(J)
, we have
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
is anticomplete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
. If
|N_{B_{i}}(J)|=1
, then we argue symmetrically to
Case 1
. So we may assume that
|N_{B_{i}}(J)|\geq 2
.
Let
Y\subseteq B_{i+1}\setminus N(K)
be the set of vertices that are anticomplete to
B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}
. We show that
Y
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. Suppose not. Choose a vertex
y\in Y
that has a non-neighbor in
B_{i+2}
with
N_{B_{i}\cup B_{i+2}}(y)
is minimum. By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
and
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
are complete to
B_{i+2}
and so
y
is anticomplete to
K\cup J
.
By Lemmas
8.1
(2) and
8.10
,
y
is minimum in
B_{i+1}
. It follows that
d(y)\leq\omega
, a contradiction. So
Y
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. By Lemma
8.10
,
B_{i+1}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
.

We then show that
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
is complete to
B_{i-2}
. By Lemma
8.10
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
and
N_{B_{i-1}}(z)
is complete to
B_{i-2}
. If
x\in N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
has a non-neighbor
x^{\prime}\in B_{i-2}
, then
x^{\prime}-q_{i-2}-x-K-N_{B_{i+1}}(K)-N_{B_{i+1}}(J)-J
is an induced sequence. So
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
is complete to
B_{i-2}
.

We now show that
K
is complete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
. As
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(z)\subseteq N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(z)
is complete to
K
. If
x\in N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
has a non-neighbor
x^{\prime}\in K
, then
x^{\prime}-w_{K}-x-q_{i-2}-q_{i+2}-N_{B_{i+1}}(J)-J
is an induced sequence. So
K
is complete to
N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
. We then show that
J
is complete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. If
y\in N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
has a non-neighbor
y^{\prime}\in J
, then
y^{\prime}-w_{J}-y-N_{B_{i+1}}(K)-K-N_{B_{i-1}}(z)-q_{i-2}
is an induced sequence. So
J
is complete to
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
.

We now show that
K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
does not contain a maximum clique. Let
M
be a maximum clique in
K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
. Since
K\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
is a clique and
|N_{B_{i-1}}(K)|>2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, we have
|K|<\omega-2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil-1
. Hence, we obtain
|M\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(K)|>2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{1}{4}\omega\right\rceil
. Then,
N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
and the claim holds.

By the definition of
z
and Lemma
8.10
, there is a vertex in
N_{B_{i-1}}(z)
complete to
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i}
. We set this vertex to be
q_{i-1}
. Then
q_{i-1}\in N_{B_{i-1}}(K)
. Let
a\in N_{B_{i-1}}(w_{J}^{\prime})
. Then
a
is complete to
J
. Let
q_{i}^{\prime}\in N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus\{q_{i}\}
be maximal over
N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus\{q_{i}\}
.
Let
b\in B_{i}\setminus N(J)
be a maximal vertex over
B_{i}\setminus N(J)
. Let
c\in N_{B_{i+1}}(w_{K}^{\prime})
. Then
c
is complete to
K
. Let
q_{j}^{\prime}\in B_{j}
be the second largest vertex in
B_{j}
for
j\in\{i-2,i+2\}
. By Lemma
13.4
, we have
q^{\prime}_{i+2}
is complete to
B_{i-2}
and
q^{\prime}_{i-2}
is complete to
B_{i+2}
. If
q^{\prime}_{i-2}
has a non-neighbor in
B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
, then a minimal vertex
x
in
B_{i-1}\setminus N(K)
has
N(x)\setminus B_{i-2}
is a clique and
N_{B_{i-2}}(x)=\{q_{i-2}\}
. So
d(x)\leq\omega
, a contradiction. So
q^{\prime}_{i-2}
is complete to
B_{i-1}
.

We set
F=(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3},F_{4},F_{5},O)
as follows:

•

F_{1}=\{q_{i-2},q_{i-2}^{\prime}\}
,

•

F_{2}=\{q_{i-1},a\}
,

•

F_{3}=\{q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},b\}
,

•

F_{4}=\{c,q_{i+1}\}
.

•

F_{5}=\{q_{i+2},q_{i+2}^{\prime}\}
,

•

O=\{w_{K},w_{J}\}
.

We can assign color
1
to
q_{i}
and
q_{i+2}
, color
2
to
q_{i-2}
,
w_{K}
and
w_{J}
, color
3
to
q_{i-2}^{\prime}
,
q_{i}^{\prime}
and
c
, color
4
to
a
,
b
and
q_{i+2}^{\prime}
, and color
5
to
q_{i-1}
and
q_{i+1}
. So
F
is
5
-colorable.

Next, we show that every maximal clique of size
\omega-j
with
j\in\{0,1,2,3\}
of
G
contains at least
4-j
vertices from
F
. Let
M
be a maximal clique of
G
. We consider the following cases depending on all possible locations of
M
. If
M
is contained in one of
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i-1}
,
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
and
B_{i-2}\cup B_{i+2}
,
M
contains four vertices of
F
.

(i)
M\subseteq K\cup N_{H}(K)

Since
M\subseteq K\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
is a clique,
M
contains
\{w_{K},a,q_{i-1},q_{i}\}
. Hence, we can assume that
M\subseteq K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
.
Since
K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)\cup\{q_{i}\}
does not contain a maximum clique and
w_{K},q_{i},c
are universal in
K\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(K)
, we obtain
w_{K},q_{i},c\in M
.

(ii)
M\subseteq J\cup N_{H}(J)

Note that
q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},w_{J}
are universal in
J\cup N_{H}(J)
. We first assume that
M\subseteq J\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. Since
N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
is complete to
J\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)
,
q_{i+1}
is universal in
J\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i+1}}(J)
. So
w_{J},q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i+1}\in M
. Now, we may assume that
M\subseteq J\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)
. Since
a
is complete to
J
, we obtain
w_{J},a,q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
.

(iii)
M\subseteq B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}

Note that
q_{i-1}
and
q_{i}
are universal in
B_{i-1}\cup B_{i}
. If
M=B_{i-1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
, then
M
is not a maximum clique and
q_{i-1},q_{i},a\in M
. So we may assume that
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\})\neq\emptyset
. We first assume that
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus N(J))=\emptyset
and this implies that
M\cap(N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus\{q_{i}\})\neq\emptyset
and so
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
. Since
a
is universal in
B_{i-1}\cup N_{B_{i}}(J)
, we have
a\in M
. Hence, we may assume that
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus N(J))\neq\emptyset
and this implies that
b\in M
.
Since
q^{\prime}_{i}
has a neighbor in
N_{B_{i-1}}(J)
,
N_{B_{i-1}}(b)\subseteq N_{B_{i-1}}(q^{\prime}_{i})
and so
q^{\prime}_{i}\in M
.

(iv)
M\subseteq B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}

Note that
q_{i}
and
q_{i+1}
are universal in
B_{i}\cup B_{i+1}
. If
M=B_{i+1}\cup\{q_{i}\}
, then
M
is not a maximum clique and
q_{i},q_{i+1},c\in M
. So we may assume that
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus\{q_{i}\})\neq\emptyset
. We first assume that
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus N(J))=\emptyset
and this implies that
M\cap(N_{B_{i}}(J)\setminus\{q_{i}\})\neq\emptyset
and
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
. So
M\cap N_{B_{i+1}}(K)=\emptyset
and we can assume that
|M|=\omega
.
As
N_{B_{i}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(z)
is a clique and
|N_{B_{i-1}}(J)\cup N_{B_{i-1}}(z)|>2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
, we obtain
|M\cap(B_{i+1}\setminus N(K))|>2\cdot\left\lceil\frac{\omega}{4}\right\rceil
. Then
B_{i+1}\cup B_{i+2}
is a clique of size larger than
\omega
. Hence,
M
is not a maximum clique and
q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i+1}\in M
. Now we may assume that
M\cap(B_{i}\setminus N(J))\neq\emptyset
and so
b\in M
. Since
N_{B_{i-1}}(b)\subseteq N_{B_{i-1}}(q^{\prime}_{i})
, we have
q_{i}^{\prime}\in M
. Hence,
M
contains
\{q_{i},q_{i}^{\prime},q_{i+1},b\}
.

Therefore,
F
is a
(4,5)
-good subgraph.
∎

14
Concluding Remarks

In this paper, we prove that
f(n)=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}n\right\rceil
is the optimal
\chi
-bounding function for the class of (
P_{7}
, even-hole)-free graphs, which generalizes the result of Karthick and Maffary
[
10
]
on (
P_{6}
, even-hole)-free graphs. We feel that
f(n)=2n-1
may not be the optimal
\chi
-bounding function for even-hole-free graphs, as this comes merely from the existence of
f
-small vertices. Equal-size blowups of
C_{5}
suggest that

f(n)=\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}n\right\rceil
may be the optimal
\chi
-bounding function for the class of even-hole-free graphs and we conjecture this should be the case.

Conjecture 14.1
.

For every even-hole-free graph
G
,
\chi(G)\leq\left\lceil\frac{5}{4}\omega(G)\right\rceil
.

Chen, Xu and Xu
[
2
]
shows that the conjecture is true for (cap, even-hole)-free graphs.
Theorem
1.1
shows that the conjecture is true for (
P_{7}
, even-hole)-free graphs.
Recently, there has been a series of work on tree-width and tree-independence number of even-hole-free graphs (see
[
3
,
5
]
for instance). The techniques developed there may offer new insights and tools to attack this conjecture.
